01 / Build contract

Start here: complete reconstruction contract

This document is the single engineering handover for Evidence Splitter. It contains both implementation guides and exact code snapshots, including the Rust lockfile. Read it offline by opening this file; no web server, login, model subscription, CDN, font download or external JavaScript is required. Its own JavaScript never sends document contents anywhere. The application described here does contact the configured model only after user consent.

Two ways to use this handover

For a human maintainer: read the workflow and setup, choose Python or Rust, inspect the corresponding guide, then use the source explorer for the implementation. The troubleshooting tables distinguish model abstention from transport failures. The code is included to eliminate ambiguity about algorithms, dependencies and flags.

For an AI implementing or restoring the application: copy the implementation contract below and retrieve the exact source bundle. Restore the files with the included standard-library script; there is no need to regenerate thousands of lines from prose. If making changes, work against those files and the specified invariants. A smaller model with a limited context window should receive the compact contract and only the relevant source module, not unrelated historical discussion. The complete code is the authoritative snapshot; the prose explains why it exists.

Current work and verification boundary

The Python fixes, Rust rewrite and handover were prepared locally. Only the Python fixes and Python Markdown handover are authorized for publication; the Rust code and this HTML remain local. Earlier development included offline tests, a local fake-API terminal smoke, and local timing measurements. After the user's latest instruction, no further application tests, builds, benchmarks or model calls were performed. The final allocation/I/O optimizations are source edits, not execution-verified performance claims. Historical results refer to the earlier snapshot only.

A dedicated index.html under a generated run is an evidence-review page; this rust/index.html is engineering documentation. Keep those roles distinct.

Compact instruction for a reconstructing AI

Implement/restore Evidence Splitter using the exact source files embedded in this HTML.
The bundle is JSON in script#source-bundle. Each item has path, UTF-8 content, and SHA-256.
Do not infer business rules from example names. Never hardcode sample hosts/services.
Do not change original PDFs. Only the Python fixes/documentation are authorized to be pushed. Do not publish Rust,
deploy, or send real evidence without separate authorization. The current request explicitly excludes executing tests,
benchmarks, builds and model calls; documented verification commands are for a future owner.

ENTRY: Python cli.py OR Rust rust/src/main.rs. Both are local terminal apps.
INPUT: exactly one existing PDF; accept typed, quoted or shell-escaped dropped paths.
PREVIEW: local first-page verbatim excerpt; image-capable terminal may show a thumbnail.
CONFIG: runtime API URL, hidden in-memory key, exact model ID or explicit service auto.
API: OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions only; HTTPS remote, HTTP loopback only.
AUTH: Bearer or api-key. Never print/save credentials or raw provider errors.
CONSENT: required before HTTP, including catalog. Images never enter model requests.
FLOW: PDF -> local source.html -> validated compact text -> LLM routing -> separate
      process HTML -> selected-process HTML read/validated on each chat question.
DISCOVERY: default automatic. Infer primary identity from headings/identifying fields.
OPTIONAL: exact target payload with >=2 distinct nonempty names, <=200 characters each.
LAYOUT: text coordinates, original pages, raster images, deterministic source-unit IDs.
SEGMENT: recognized opening/close markers with continuations; otherwise page candidates.
FURNITURE: explicit known whitelist, audited separately; never strip repeated table rows.
ROUTING: assigned/uncertain/mixed/unmatched. Only assigned has a non-null valid target.
          Keep same identity's start/stop together; do not merge similar identifiers.
          Mixed inseparable evidence is withheld, not duplicated into several groups.
EXPORT: original escaped text and local PNGs; model does not write or rewrite evidence.
        source.html, process-NN-slug.html, withheld.html, furniture.html, manifest.json,
        index.html, images/*.png. Complete decision coverage is mandatory.
CHAT: only selected group and its own scoped history. Re-read its HTML; no fallback.
      Partial questions: answer supported parts, explicitly identify what is not established.
      Distinguish separate services under a host. State changes are not inherently conflicts.
      Never infer facts visible only in images or claim zero hallucinations.
CONTEXT: count serialized message characters (default 24000), not tokens. Bounded batches
         visit every selected unit; preserve section/page/ID. No silent text truncation.
         At most four prior turns, bounded further to a quarter of budget; report trimming.
         Label batch answers; no unchecked global synthesis or process-wide absence claim.
CITATIONS: exact selected text unit, correct section/page, nonempty exact substring quote.
           Never cite image pixels. One citation correction; reject foreign/fabricated refs.
           Parent-ID normalization requires a unique matching quote/section/page.
REPAIR: one JSON/schema repair per completion, no blind transport retry. Up to four calls
        per chat batch when both JSON and semantic correction are needed.
IMAGES: local membership+path containment, terminal native graphics or honest viewer/path.
COMMANDS: /ls /evidence /images [number|ID] /html /chunk N /all /clear /back /process /quit.
ISOLATION: histories keyed by process and optional chunk. Withheld evidence cannot be chatted.
RUST: native Poppler subprocesses; no Python runtime. 1-4 scoped page-range workers,
      merge in page order before segmentation; source IDs independent of worker count.
VERSIONS: Python uses PyMuPDF spans; Rust uses Poppler units and rust-poppler-v1 manifest.
          Do not mix runs, IDs, manifests or extracted assets across implementations.
SOURCE OF TRUTH: embedded source snapshot + Cargo.lock + requirements.txt.

File layout to create

project/
  README.md                  short entry points, links back to this document
  .gitignore
  requirements.txt
  targets.example.json
  cli.py                     Python terminal interface
  pipeline.py                Python backend
  test_pipeline.py           existing offline regressions; optional to run later
  rust/
    index.html               this single documentation artifact
    Cargo.toml
    Cargo.lock
    src/
      main.rs                Rust interface and lifecycle
      client.rs              schemas, text-only transport, strict result validation
      pipeline.rs            extraction, grouping, export, isolated chat
      tests.rs               existing offline fixtures/regressions

The original historical HANDOFF.md and user's split-pdfs/ remain in the original repository; neither is needed to restore or run the app with a user-supplied PDF. Generated runs, binaries, .venv, API keys, production PDFs and user conversation history are deliberately absent from the source bundle. The guide does not embed itself recursively. When restoring, copy this document into the restored rust/index.html if you want the README documentation link available there.

State machine and responsibilities

State Owner Successful transition Error behavior
Choose input CLI One normalized readable PDF Retry interactive path or exit for invalid explicit argument
Preview Local converter Text excerpt/optional thumbnail Local parse/viewer error; no API request
Configure CLI + Client Valid URL/auth/model, affirmative disclosure consent Stop before network if not approved
Convert Extraction Positioned units, source HTML, local images Converter/file error; incomplete output is not a successful run
Baseline export Exporter State not classified, all evidence reviewable Never imply classification succeeded
Classify Model + semantic validator One valid decision per original section Stop on invalid response/systemic provider failure
Publish local result Exporter State complete, process files and manifest Partial write remains incomplete; no atomic publication guarantee
Select CLI One group or withheld review Invalid selection does not change scope
Ask Chat Scope validated, every selected unit covered, citations checked No cross-group fallback; safe abstention or actionable error
Show reference Local viewer Selected image resolved inside run images No API call; missing/foreign image fails locally
Exit CLI Files remain, memory-only key/history released with process New session requires fresh configuration; no resume feature

Exact model contracts: result instances, not schemas

The production schemas are included in each backend. These fictional examples illustrate the result shape; they are not fixed process names or automatic answers. Every field is required and extra fields are rejected.

A classification result:

{
  "section_id": "s-EXACT_ID_FROM_REQUEST",
  "status": "assigned",
  "target": "invoice-flow",
  "confidence": 0.9,
  "reason": "The identifying text explicitly names this process."
}

A withheld result uses uncertain, mixed or unmatched, with target: null. Confidence must be finite and between zero and one. Discovery additionally checks that an assigned label actually occurs in the section at an identifier boundary. Optional target mode checks exact membership in the supplied labels. A valid shape alone never authorizes a wrong section ID or inconsistent status/target.

A factual answer with a missing component:

{
  "answer": "A stop is documented. A start is not established by the supplied text.",
  "abstain": false,
  "citations": [
    {
      "section_id": "s-EXACT_SECTION_ID",
      "block_id": "EXACT_TEXT_UNIT_ID",
      "page": 7,
      "quote": "Operation: stop"
    }
  ]
}

This answer is accepted only if that exact text-unit ID belongs to the selected batch and original page 7, and contains that quote. The IDs shown above are placeholders and would fail on a real run. A multipart answer must not invent missing events, merge multiple services, assume chronology without evidence, or interpret missing evidence as proof an event never happened.

An unsupported answer:

{"answer":"","abstain":true,"citations":[]}

When the model chooses abstention, the CLI shows locally generated guidance rather than trusting arbitrary factual prose inside its answer field. The reported “selected evidence did not support a reliable answer” was this path, not an HTTP exception. The revised instruction permits partial useful answers, but a text model can still lack evidence. No source-code shortcut substitutes a canned answer for the real model.

A concrete HTTP request

POST <configured-base>/chat/completions
Authorization: Bearer <hidden-key>
Content-Type: application/json

Use api-key: <hidden-key> instead when that header mode is selected. A full custom chat URL is used as configured. Query parameters remain after the route. A body is:

{
  "model": "<exact-runtime-model-id>",
  "messages": [
    {"role":"system","content":"<instruction plus the strict result schema>"},
    {"role":"user","content":"<serialized payload containing only compact text>"}
  ],
  "response_format": {"type":"json_object"},
  "temperature": 0,
  "max_tokens": 2048
}

json_schema mode uses {type, json_schema: {name: "result", strict: true, schema}} instead. Classification's payload contains one evidence object plus boundary_note and known_groups or targets. Chat's payload contains process, scope, an evidence array, scoped history, and question. Images are text reference IDs, not pixels, attachments, file paths or data URLs. Normalized parent_block_id is metadata; cite the block's id in the response's block_id field.

Deterministic rules for future changes

  1. Retain every original extracted unit exactly once in sections or furniture.
  2. Keep stable ownership from source to HTML to model payload to citation.
  3. A missing or changed selected HTML must fail before HTTP. Never fall back to all-document context or stale in-memory text.
  4. Preserve the selected group's confidentiality relative to other groups.
  5. Never loosen citation checks to make a model appear more helpful.
  6. Do not silently change grouping level or reinterpret example identifiers.
  7. Keep call retries bounded. A provider outage is not evidence uncertainty.
  8. Optimize local code and copying independently of model behavior. Do not claim a faster API completion merely because the frontend is Rust.
  9. Conversion may run concurrently; dependent discovery naming stays sequential.
  10. A new source snapshot changes the bundle hashes; update documentation when code changes. These hashes detect accidental drift, not establish publisher identity.
02 / Python handover

Python implementation: engineering and internship handover

Updated 23 October 2026. Read this alongside pipeline.py, cli.py, and test_pipeline.py. This document describes the implementation, including the chat changes made during this handover. HANDOFF.md is historical context: later user requirements supersede its fixed-target default and its earlier exclusion of Rust. The Python implementation is retained as an independently runnable reference.

Current delivery status

The final code changes were not built, tested or benchmarked, following the user's instruction. Earlier test results describe the preceding snapshot, not the final allocation optimization edits. Python chat packing now extends batches in place, rolling back only the overflowing unit, while retaining exact budget checks. Each completion reuses a Draft 2020-12 schema validator across its bounded attempts. Invalid context-budget flags are rejected before preview/provider configuration. Rust implementation and its consolidated HTML handover are maintained separately under rust/; publication of this Python handover does not publish that directory.

1. Purpose, current contract, and boundaries

The application turns one PDF into HTML, asks a configured text LLM to classify logical evidence sections, exports one independent HTML per discovered group, and answers questions using only the selected group's exported evidence.

The default interaction is: PDF path → local preview → API configuration and consent → conversion → automatic grouping → process list → questions/images. Users do not need to name processes in advance. --payload is an optional restriction to supplied names. Names and grouping levels come from document text; there are no hardcoded production process names. The server/service distinction matters: a model may choose a host as the primary entity, and that group can then contain multiple services. Review this interpretation before downstream use.

Images are evidence for human inspection. No image pixels, data URLs, attachments, or image paths go to the LLM. A text-only model cannot read commands or states that exist solely inside screenshots. There is no OCR. Native terminal image display does not change the model's capabilities.

The code validates identities, ownership, exact quotes and pages. It cannot prove that a classification or a paraphrase is semantically correct. Do not describe the system as having zero hallucinations or use model confidence as measured accuracy.

2. Setup from a clean machine

Requirements: Python 3.10+, a terminal, and installed dependencies in requirements.txt. The code has been tested with the repository's Python 3.13 virtual environment; other declared Python versions need their own compatibility checks. Network access is needed to install packages and for an approved model connection, but not for extraction or image viewing.

python3 -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.venv/bin/python cli.py

Windows activation/launch equivalent:

py -m venv .venv
.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.venv\Scripts\python.exe cli.py

Run commands from the repository root. Output goes to runs/ relative to the working directory. No user's Desktop, home directory, provider URL, or model ID is a required deployment path. PDF paths may be typed literally, quoted, or pasted using terminal drag/drop escaping. A directory is not an input PDF.

First perform a local-only conversion:

.venv/bin/python cli.py --pdf '/path/to/evidence.pdf' --extract-only --no-browser

--no-browser exits after exports instead of entering the interactive document menu; it does not disable the earlier local preview. Review source.html, withheld.html, furniture.html, and index.html in the printed run directory. Use ordinary filesystem access controls for these files: they contain the PDF's text and images. Nothing encrypts them automatically.

3. Configuration and provider onboarding

Setting Default and meaning
PDF Prompt or --pdf; one existing PDF
API URL Prompt; HTTPS base URL or full compatible chat URL
Key Hidden terminal prompt; retained in memory, never intentionally persisted
Model Prompt; exact provider ID or explicit router auto
--auth bearer; alternatively api-key header
--response-format json_object; alternatively strict json_schema
--url-type auto; base appends chat route, chat preserves full custom URL
--context-chars 24000 serialized message characters; minimum CLI value 4000
--payload Optional JSON file with at least two distinct target strings
--extract-only No model configuration or requests
EVIDENCE_IMAGE_PROTOCOL Optional kitty, iterm2, or none override

The character budget is not a token budget. Different languages, escaping, and models have different tokenization. Set it conservatively below the selected model's context capacity, allowing space for output. Increasing it does not grant the model a larger context window. The output allowance is 2048 tokens per call.

Base URLs append /chat/completions without adding /v1 implicitly. A URL already ending in /chat/completions is preserved. Query parameters remain after the path, including API version parameters. For a custom complete route, use --url-type chat. The catalog route is /models under the same base; a custom nonstandard chat URL cannot imply a model catalog, so supply an exact model ID.

The implemented wire protocol is OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions. A generic URL field does not implement native Anthropic Messages, Gemini generateContent, OpenAI Responses, or arbitrary proprietary protocols. Such APIs need a compatible gateway or a deliberately tested adapter. HTTPS can be remote; HTTP is accepted only for loopback addresses. Redirects, URL credentials, common secret query names, fragments, malformed ports and unsafe URL whitespace are rejected.

/models filters routes marked needsKey, exhausted, or equivalent execution status. Availability may still change after listing. auto only works if the configured service supports it. For a forwarding router, the key may be the router's unified key rather than its upstream provider's key. Never put real keys in shell arguments, examples, test fixtures, manifests, bug reports, or commits.

4. Files and ownership

File Responsibility / likely maintainer changes
pipeline.py Extraction, segmentation, HTML provenance, HTTP, classification, exports, chat
cli.py Input, local preview, progress, menus, terminal images and viewer launch
test_pipeline.py Real temporary PDF fixtures and mocked HTTP regressions
requirements.txt Reproducible pinned Python dependencies
targets.example.json Optional fixed-label payload format
README.md Short user entry point
HANDOFF.md Original brief and historical decisions
PYTHON_MAINTENANCE.md This technical operations guide
rust/ Independent native implementation; see its own handover

There is no web server, database, scheduler, queue, vector store, model training, or background ingestion process. A run is local files plus in-memory application state. A second user should run their own process and output directory. Importing a manifest and resuming a prior Python run is not currently a CLI feature.

5. End-to-end execution and failure boundaries

  1. main() parses flags; select_pdf() normalizes and validates the path.
  2. preview_pdf() reads the first page locally. The shown excerpt is verbatim extraction, not an AI summary. Graphics-capable terminals get a local thumbnail; other interactive terminals offer the system viewer.
  3. configure() gathers endpoint, key and model, then asks for explicit consent. No evidence request precedes that approval. Catalog requests also require it.
  4. extract() creates the run and image directory, validates PDF/page metadata, hashes the file, extracts positioned units, calls segment(), and writes source.html before classification starts.
  5. export(..., state='not classified') creates a reviewable local baseline. All sections are withheld; this is not successful routing.
  6. classify() reads and validates source.html, routes eligible sections one at a time, validates each result, and reports real completed section counts.
  7. Only complete valid decisions are passed to export(..., state='complete'). It assembles source evidence into independent documents; the model never writes or rewrites the HTML evidence.
  8. browse() lists processes, withheld evidence, index and decisions. A question invokes Chat.ask() for the selected document. Images are resolved locally.

A provider failure stops classification rather than producing a stream of false uncertain labels. The previous local baseline remains inspectable. A filesystem failure during export can leave partial files: exports are not transactional. Check manifest.json state and all referenced files; do not infer completeness from a directory existing. New runs use new timestamp directories, avoiding reuse of old successful output as if it belonged to a failed run.

6. Data model and provenance invariants

data contains sources, pages, sections, and furniture. The manifest adds state, targets, documents, and decisions.

  • Source: filename, full SHA-256, starting original page, count, and family name.
  • Page: deterministic ID, original page number, source/family, width/height, units.
  • Text unit: ID, parent block_id, page/page ID/source, bounding box, text, spans.
  • Span: original text, rectangle, font size, flags and color.
  • Image unit: analogous provenance plus relative PNG path, no extracted text.
  • Section: ID based on its first unit, ordered units, and optional boundary note.
  • Decision: section_id, status, nullable target, confidence in [0,1], reason.

Example ID shape: d<12 hex hash>-p0007-b003-l001. b003 is a PDF block and l001 identifies a text line within it. A section adds s-. Image IDs generally end at the parent block. A file rewrite changes the hash even if its appearance is similar; IDs are not durable business identifiers across independently saved PDF versions. A _pages_11-20.pdf filename supplies original page offset 11 and must contain exactly ten pages. Without that convention, page numbering starts at one. The application cannot reconstruct omitted pages from a slice.

Every extracted unit must be owned by exactly one section or furniture. Furniture is an explicit allowlist of known headers/footers; repeated table rows are evidence and must not be dropped by a generic repetition heuristic. Source image paths must resolve inside this run's image directory before local display.

HTML wrappers carry id, data-section, data-block, and data-page. Text is escaped; images are embedded as PNG data URLs for standalone copying. read_html() parses known wrappers and checks exact text, page and ownership against in-memory source provenance. It rejects missing, duplicate, foreign or modified evidence. Titles/CSS/image bytes are not context. This detects evidence mismatches, not malicious modification of the entire source-and-manifest trust base. Never treat arbitrary downloaded manifests as authenticated evidence.

7. Segmentation and classification policy

is_heading() recognizes explicit evidence headings and sufficiently large standalone Start/Stop headings. segment() carries open sections across contiguous pages and finishes at explicit close markers. Orphans, interrupted sections, missing pages and missing close markers receive boundary notes. If the document has no recognized heading anywhere, each page is an independent candidate.

Discovery sends ordinary boundary notes to the model for conservative assessment; missing/unrelated continuation pages remain locally withheld. Optional fixed-label mode withholds every ambiguous boundary before calling the model. A mixed page with separable recognized sections can be routed independently. An unmarked page containing inseparable different identities must be withheld as mixed. More robust column/layout analysis would be a future feature, not a reason to guess today.

Discovery provides known_groups to encourage consistent names. An assigned label must occur exactly at an identifier boundary in source text, be printable, trimmed, and at most 200 characters. This prevents fabricated or partial identifiers but does not decide whether host, service or business process is the right grouping level. The model makes that choice. Review wrong groupings in the manifest before changing the segmentation rules.

Statuses: assigned requires a valid target; mixed, uncertain and unmatched require null target. No duplicate assignment to two outputs is permitted. Confidence is displayed for review; there is no confidence threshold implying correctness.

8. Chat behavior and the reported failure

The reported message was “Abstained: the selected evidence did not support a reliable answer,” for “has the process relay-44 started and stopped?” This message came from a schema-valid model response with abstain: true; it was not a network exception or an HTTP error. The previous code discarded the model's answer and showed a generic abstention. An unsupported model explanation must not become a trusted factual answer just because it is attached to an abstention.

Offline inspection of the latest saved run found a group containing explicit stop records for two services, plus health/maintenance records, without an explicit start record. This explains why the old instruction to abstain when evidence was missing could suppress the entire multipart answer. It does not prove the live model's internal reason. A useful grounded answer confirms the documented stop and states that the supplied text does not establish the start; it does not infer “never started,” nor conflate multiple services under one host.

The revised generic instruction answers supported parts of multipart questions, identifies unsupported parts, distinguishes services within a group, and does not regard start/stop at different times as inherently contradictory. It contains no special-case process names or operation-response shortcuts.

Additional robustness changes:

  • Every question re-reads only the selected process HTML and validates it.
  • Chat labels parent identifiers parent_block_id; citations should use text id.
  • A parent ID returned by a model can be normalized only when section, page and exact quote identify exactly one text line in the current request. Ambiguity fails.
  • Invalid citation semantics get one bounded correction request. Invalid quotes, foreign IDs or pages still cannot pass. Raw failed outputs are not printed.
  • Recent history is limited to four turns and at most one quarter of the budget. If older history is omitted, the CLI tells the user; evidence is not omitted.
  • When the selected process is too large, every original unit is visited in bounded batches. A section can be split at unit boundaries. Every request stays within the configured serialized character budget and leaves room for corrections.
  • Batch answers are explicitly labeled, individually cited and displayed together. They are not synthesized into an unsupported global conclusion. A missing fact in one batch does not mean it is absent from other batches. Cross-batch conflicts and chronology require reviewing the returned evidence; this is a deliberate limitation of the small implementation.
  • Any invalid batch causes the entire question to abstain. API failures propagate without presenting a partial result as success. No failed batch is silently skipped.
  • An individual huge line/question still produces an actionable budget error; it is never silently truncated. Use /clear, a smaller question, or a suitable higher budget. /chunk N remains an explicit smaller scope with separate history.

History keys are (target, chunk). Different process chats, full process chat and section chat do not share histories. Answers and history are memory-only. All factual answers require nonempty validated citations. An image reference is never a valid quoted text citation. Abstaining outputs are replaced with local guidance rather than displaying unchecked assertions from the model.

9. HTTP and structured result handling

Only Client uses HTTP. request() enforces consent, allowed URLs, auth headers, no redirects, ten-second connection timeout and ninety-second read timeout. Requests has no application-level automatic retry here. Provider bodies and raw transport exceptions are suppressed to avoid exposing secrets or source text.

complete() constructs system/user messages, embeds the JSON schema, requests a result instance, sets temperature zero and output limit 2048, then parses the Chat Completions envelope. A single enclosing JSON fence is tolerated. Arbitrary prose around JSON, nonfinite numbers, extra fields and wrong types are rejected. JSON/schema failure receives one correction; truncated output and transport errors do not. Chat citation repair is a separate semantic stage, so a batch can consume up to four HTTP completions when both repairs are needed. Classification gets at most two completions per eligible section. Budget checks precede each request.

10. Output and terminal operation

Artifact Meaning
source.html All converted evidence plus audited furniture
process-NN-<slug>.html Only the selected target's assigned original units
withheld.html Everything not assigned
furniture.html Explicitly recognized non-evidence text, retained for audit
index.html Links and classification decision table
manifest.json Full original provenance and decisions; no credentials
images/*.png Locally extracted image references

Blank areas in a process HTML preserve source positioning where other evidence was removed. Text spans/raster images are retained; fonts, vector drawings, arbitrary rotations and complicated transforms are not guaranteed pixel-perfect.

Menu: number/name selects a group, w inspects withheld, r shows decisions, i opens index, /ls lists files. Within a group: /evidence, /images [number or ID], /html, /chunk N, /all, /clear, /back, /process, /help, /quit. /ls uses Python filesystem listing; it does not execute untrusted shell strings.

Ghostty/Kitty use Kitty graphics; iTerm2/WezTerm use OSC 1337. Redirected stdout and unsupported terminals receive paths instead. Multiplexers may block protocols; use a directly supported terminal or a deliberately configured override. Missing images produce local errors. Citation-associated images display after answers without sending pixels to the API. A successful escape write is not proof of visual display; verify on the actual user's terminal.

11. Troubleshooting decision table

Symptom Check and recovery
Model abstains /evidence: is the requested fact present as text in this selected group? Try a specific supported part. Check screenshots via /images. Review grouping and missing PDF slices. Do not remove validation to force answers.
Unsupported/foreign citations Confirm line IDs and exact quotes; one repair is automatic. Repeated failures suggest model/prompt compatibility; inspect a sanitized synthetic reproduction.
Context budget error /clear; shorten question; choose /chunk N; increase --context-chars only within actual model capacity. Check for exceptionally long individual lines.
HTTP 401 / 403 Check router versus provider key and --auth; verify access. Never print the key.
HTTP 404 Correct complete route, base prefix and exact model ID.
HTTP 400 Check structured response support, model parameters and context limits. Try the explicitly supported JSON mode.
HTTP 429 Stop; inspect quota/rate limits and retry later or choose available model.
Redirect refused Enter the final trusted endpoint directly.
Network/TLS failure Confirm endpoint, certificates, proxy and reachability; do not disable TLS checks.
Truncated model output Reduce scope or choose appropriate model/output capacity; inspect 2048-token setting in complete().
Invalid JSON/schema after repair Provider may echo schema or add fields; use synthetic data to evaluate compatibility.
HTML modified/missing/foreign Reconvert source. Do not bypass integrity checks or use another process's HTML.
Wrong groups Inspect decisions and original text; adjust generic segmentation/prompt or provide explicit payload.
All image-only pages withheld Expected without OCR; add a tested local OCR stage only as a separate requirement.
Image path but no graphic Terminal/protocol limitation, not LLM failure; use compatible terminal or local viewer.
Generic local failure Check PDF validity, output write permission, disk, dependency versions; reproduce using synthetic fixture under a debugger without real keys.

Collect for an issue: version/commit, OS/Python, command flags without secrets, question, selected label, safe error text, source section/page IDs, run state and a sanitized small reproduction. Do not attach production PDFs, manifests or raw HTTP headers by default. Ask the owner which evidence can be shared.

12. Tests and maintenance workflow

.venv/bin/python -B -m unittest -v test_pipeline
.venv/bin/python test_pipeline.py --make-fixture /tmp/evidence-fixture.pdf

An earlier Python snapshot passed 44 tests; the final allocation edits were not retested. Tests create actual PDFs in temporary directories and mock only HTTP. They cover mixed pages, cross-page sections, repeated rows, source offsets, all-block conservation, image ownership, real HTML export/read-back, modified/missing HTML, provider errors, authentication modes, URL/query composition, malformed JSON, schema errors, invalid IDs/status pairs, quotation validation, isolation, explicit chunk scope, batch coverage and history bounds. Graphics tests inspect protocol bytes, not a screenshot of terminal rendering. CLI tests prove automatic discovery has no target-name prompt. The mock oracle is test data, not a production classifier.

For a segmentation change, first create a PDF fixture showing the failing layout; assert every unit appears exactly once in sections/furniture and no process acquires foreign evidence. For a chat change, inspect serialized mock request bodies and assert no other process, image bytes or paths are sent. Include accepted and rejected citations, not just a golden successful response. For a provider change, mock HTTP errors/redirects and response envelopes before a live synthetic smoke test.

Run the full Python suite after shared backend edits. Record the exact test result, not a copied historical count. A passed mock suite does not establish live model accuracy. Live reproduction of the reported abstention requires the user's provider and approved synthetic/sanitized data; no key was available during this handover.

13. Operations, upgrades, and next-intern checklist

  1. Start by reading the short README and this guide, then run all tests.
  2. Make one local extraction and inspect text, images, order, page numbering and withheld boundaries against the original PDF.
  3. Use a controlled synthetic PDF with known outcomes for the first provider test. Record model ID, response mode and reviewed expected answers without secrets.
  4. Test a compound question where one part is missing: confirm the supported part is answered with quotes and the missing part is not invented.
  5. Test an unsupported question and foreign image/citation request: expect refusal.
  6. Check terminal graphics on the deployment terminal and fallback behavior.
  7. Measure extraction, export and model wait separately. Do not attribute network latency to Python or assume a language rewrite improves provider responses.
  8. Review dependency upgrades one at a time. Keep pinned versions and regenerate fixtures only intentionally; extraction changes can alter unit IDs/boundaries.
  9. Back up source PDFs and required run directories according to the organization's policy. Delete old runs explicitly when permitted; the app has no retention job.
  10. Retain the previous code/environment for rollback; new code should use a new run. Never mix old manifests with regenerated HTML or images.

Known extension points: local OCR with provenance, more general layout segmentation, explicit grouping-level configuration, resume/import validation, cross-batch synthesis with conflict verification, context token counting per model, safe structured diagnostics, measured accuracy evaluation, transactional exports, and controlled parallel classification for fixed targets. They are future work, not features to imply already exist. Keep the current minimal design until a measured problem warrants additional infrastructure.

03 / Rust handover

Rust Evidence Splitter — implementation and operations handover

Updated 23 October 2026. This is an independent Rust application, not a launcher for the Python code. It implements PDF conversion, HTML provenance, automatic process discovery, independent exports, selected-process chat, checked citations, and local terminal image references. The Python implementation remains in the repository as a runnable reference and rollback option; see the Python chapter in this document.

1. Start here

The input is one PDF path, typed or pasted by dragging the PDF into the terminal. The application previews it locally, collects a compatible API endpoint/key/model, converts the PDF, discovers groups, lists their HTML files, and lets the user select a group for questions. There are no predefined production process names and no mandatory target-name prompt. A group's title may identify a host, service or other entity according to the document; users must review that grouping choice.

Runtime requirements:

  • The built evidence-splitter executable for the target operating system/CPU.
  • Poppler tools on PATH: pdfinfo and pdftohtml for conversion; pdftotext and pdftoppm for the default local preview. --no-preview needs pdfinfo and pdftohtml.
  • A writable output directory; default runs/ under the current working directory.
  • An interactive terminal for hidden API-key entry. --extract-only also works noninteractively and never contacts a model.
  • A provider implementing OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions with JSON output.

Build requirements: Rust/Cargo with edition 2024 support and a toolchain compatible with Cargo.lock. The checked build used Rust/Cargo 1.95.0. This project does not claim that every older edition-2024 compiler can build the locked dependencies. Use that tested toolchain initially, then evaluate upgrades in a branch.

On macOS, if using Homebrew:

brew install poppler

On Debian/Ubuntu:

sudo apt-get install poppler-utils

On Windows, install a trusted Poppler distribution appropriate to the organization and put its bin directory on PATH. Build with the native Rust toolchain. Linux and Windows commands describe intended portability; this handover's execution checks were on macOS. Verify viewer launch, terminal graphics, paths and Poppler availability on each deployment platform.

From the repository root:

cargo build --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --release --locked
./rust/target/release/evidence-splitter

From the rust/ directory, the equivalent commands are:

cargo build --release --locked
./target/release/evidence-splitter

Windows executable: rust\target\release\evidence-splitter.exe. Python, pip, PyMuPDF, a Python virtual environment and the synthetic dataset are not runtime requirements. A Rust executable still needs Poppler; shipping the executable alone does not install that external toolchain.

2. First validation and normal use

Before configuring a model, verify local extraction:

./rust/target/release/evidence-splitter \
  --pdf '/path/to/evidence.pdf' \
  --extract-only --no-browser

For unattended extraction without preview:

./rust/target/release/evidence-splitter \
  --pdf '/path/to/evidence.pdf' \
  --extract-only --no-browser --no-preview \
  --output '/path/to/output-parent'

Open the printed index.html and compare source text, page numbering, positions, images and withheld boundaries with the PDF. An extraction-only run has state not classified; it is not a classified result. No empty group list should be interpreted as evidence that no processes exist.

For normal classification/chat, omit --extract-only. Enter the API base or full chat URL, a key at the hidden prompt, and the exact model ID. auto is an explicit routing request to a service that supports that name; it is not universal model discovery. /models lists available IDs before choosing one. Consent is required before network access: a localhost router can forward text to an upstream service.

Default authentication is Bearer. Alternatives:

./rust/target/release/evidence-splitter \
  --auth api-key --response-format json_schema --url-type chat

Example optional fixed-target mode:

./rust/target/release/evidence-splitter --payload targets.example.json

Run that last command from the repository root, or use an appropriate payload path. Automatic discovery remains the default. Do not copy sample labels into production routing code.

3. CLI contract

Flag Meaning
--pdf PATH One PDF; otherwise prompt for a typed/dropped path
--payload PATH JSON containing at least two distinct exact target names
--auth bearer|api-key Header convention; default Bearer
--response-format json_object|json_schema Provider JSON mode; both validated locally
--url-type auto|base|chat Interpret endpoint; explicit chat preserves custom full URL
--context-chars N Serialized message-character budget; default 24000, CLI minimum 4000
--extract-only Local conversion/export; no model configuration or API requests
--no-browser Exit after export instead of entering the document menu
--no-preview Skip local first-page preview
--pdf-workers N 1–4 independent conversion ranges; default 4, capped by available CPUs/pages
--output PATH Parent directory for a uniquely created run; default runs
--help, --version Usage and package version

The default preview is first-page locally extracted text, filename/path and size. It is not an LLM-generated summary. Compatible terminals additionally render a local thumbnail; other interactive terminals offer the PDF's system viewer. Page and section counts are reported after conversion. Unlike the Python preview, the Rust preview does not query the whole PDF's page count before conversion.

At the document menu: select a number or exact group name, w for withheld, r for routing results, i for index, /ls for files, q or /quit to leave.

Inside a document:

Command Effect
/evidence Show original text units, IDs, pages and section/chunk numbers
/images List and select local image references
/images N or /images ID Resolve and display only an image in this document
/html Open selected HTML in system viewer/browser
/chunk N Explicitly restrict questions to one section with its own history
/all Restore full selected-process evidence scope
/clear Clear only this process/scope conversation
/ls List process HTML labels, names, section counts, sizes and absolute paths
/process, /back Return to document selection
/help, /quit Command help or exit

Simple image requests such as “show me the reference image” are local commands. Other questions go to the configured model only when a classified process is selected. Withheld evidence is reviewable but not a process-chat context.

/ls is implemented using Rust filesystem operations rather than a platform shell. This works on systems without Unix ls and avoids executing document text as a command. Viewers and converters are launched using separate argument values, never an interpolated shell command.

4. Small codebase map

File Responsibility
Cargo.toml Package metadata and direct dependencies
Cargo.lock Exact resolved dependency graph; retain with the executable project
src/main.rs CLI, configuration, local preview, protocol display, menus, run lifecycle
src/client.rs Model interface, strict schemas/validation, URL construction and HTTP
src/pipeline.rs Typed evidence, Poppler extraction, segmentation, HTML, routing, export, chat
src/tests.rs Offline model oracle, PDF generator, regressions and loopback HTTP checks
README.md This setup, implementation, maintenance and operations guide

There is no web service, database, queue, vector store, AI framework, Python bridge, or bundled model. main.rs is intentionally thin relative to the shared pipeline, but contains display code so that graphics do not enter the model layer. Public functions and data structures have purpose comments; local comments explain trust and layout decisions. cargo doc --no-deps can generate source API documentation.

Dependency choices

  • quick-xml: reads Poppler's positioned XML; external DTDs are not fetched.
  • scraper: parses exported HTML locally before a model request.
  • serde/serde_json: typed records and strict JSON parsing/serialization.
  • reqwest with Rustls: TLS, connection reuse, bounded blocking HTTP suitable for a single interactive CLI. Blocking does not mean unbounded; requests have timeouts.
  • image: identifies local image formats and normalizes non-PNG output to PNG references.
  • sha2: content-derived provenance namespace.
  • base64: only for local HTML and terminal graphics, never model payloads.
  • regex: generic layout markers, identifier validation and safe filenames.
  • clap: typed CLI options and usage output.
  • dialoguer: hidden key entry.
  • shell-words: parse pasted terminal quoting without executing a shell.
  • tempfile: local disposable conversion and preview workspaces.
  • anyhow: contextual local errors; sensitive transport errors are replaced at their boundary rather than blindly formatting an entire error chain.

Adding a dependency has build, audit and distribution cost. Add one only for a measured need; do not replace this small workflow with a framework just to extend a prompt or export format.

5. Execution flow and persistence

run() chooses and previews the PDF, configures the provider if needed, creates runs/rust-<epoch-nanoseconds>-<pid>/, calls extract(), writes an unclassified baseline using pending()/export(), then calls classify() and exports complete results. If classification fails, the local baseline remains. There is no partial successful routing state. Each new run has its own directory and never overwrites a prior successful run intentionally.

Converter scratch data is created with tempfile::tempdir_in(run) and removed when conversion returns normally or with an ordinary error. A hard process kill can leave scratch files. Do not delete a directory merely because it is temporary- looking while a process may still own it. Outputs are not encrypted automatically. The program has no automatic retention or cleanup policy for completed runs.

Exports are ordinary filesystem writes, not an atomic directory transaction. An output permission/disk failure can leave partial files; verify manifest state and each referenced file rather than interpreting existence as completeness. manifest.json is written after the evidence documents and before the index. Consumers requiring transactional publication should add a final ready marker or atomic directory publication as an explicit follow-up feature.

No import/resume command is implemented. Closing a process loses conversational history and credentials. Re-run classification for a fresh interactive session; old HTML/manifests remain useful for manual review. Reusing a previous manifest for questions would require validating its complete provenance trust boundary.

6. PDF conversion and source geometry

extract() canonicalizes the source path, reads the page count through pdfinfo, and converts independent page ranges with at most four scoped worker threads. The worker count is bounded by --pdf-workers, available CPU parallelism and page count. Each worker invokes:

pdftohtml -q -xml -hidden -nomerge -noroundcoord -zoom 1 -fmt png -f FIRST -l LAST INPUT SCRATCH/evidence.xml

Each worker uses its own disposable XML/image directory. Results are joined in original page order and only then segmented, so sections crossing worker ranges remain intact. Failed or missing page ranges fail extraction; no partial source is published. Set --pdf-workers 1 for serial conversion on constrained machines. A regression compares serial/parallel source records and cross-range sections. These are arguments to Command, not shell text. -zoom 1 uses PDF point scale; -noroundcoord retains decimal positions; -nomerge keeps text units smaller for provenance. Hidden text is included, which can be useful for PDFs with existing text layers but can duplicate content in some documents. There is no added OCR. A converter process is bounded to 180 seconds, then killed and reaped. Its raw stderr is not exposed in the CLI. Check Poppler installation and PDF validity when it fails, rather than assuming an LLM error.

The XML parser reads page dimensions, font size/color, text including nested inline markup, character entities, and image rectangles/paths. It does not follow external entities or execute links. Image paths must resolve inside the conversion workspace. Existing Poppler PNGs are copied directly to run/images/<unit-id>.png; JPEG output is decoded and normalized to PNG. Avoiding unnecessary PNG decode/recompression preserves pixels and reduces redundant work. Geometry remains from the XML.

Poppler emits images and text in separate sequences. Before segmentation, units are ordered by top coordinate then left coordinate so local image references follow the surrounding evidence. This is a simple reading-order heuristic; interleaved columns, overlapping objects or rotated text may require explicit layout work. Bold text is approximated at fragment level; arbitrary inline font/style changes, vector drawings and embedded fonts are not reproduced exactly. Review unfamiliar PDF layouts against the source.

The application streams the source through a 64 KiB buffer to compute SHA-256 and uses it to namespace IDs. A filename ending _pages_START-END.pdf supplies original page offsets and must match the actual page count. Ordinary filenames number pages from one. Missing pages cannot be inferred. Never rename slices to misleading ranges.

7. Rust data contract and migration differences

Core types in pipeline.rs:

Type Important fields
Block id, block_id, page, page_id, kind, text, bbox, size, color, bold, optional image path
Page ID, original number, width/height, ordered blocks
Section ID, ordered blocks, optional boundary note
Data Source filename/hash/start, pages, sections, furniture
Decision Section ID, status, optional target, confidence, reason
Citation Section ID, text-unit ID in block_id, original page, exact quote
Answer Answer string, abstain Boolean, citations
Manifest format_version, state, targets, documents map, decisions, nested data

Rust IDs look like r<12 hex hash>-p0007-u0003. The r prefix makes the extractor namespace distinct. A section ID prefixes its first unit with s-. Unlike PyMuPDF's parent-block/line hierarchy, a Poppler text fragment is one unit, so its block_id normally equals its id. Model-facing payloads call the parent field parent_block_id to avoid confusing the citation contract.

The manifest format is rust-poppler-v1; extraction is nested under data. Python manifests use different top-level fields, span records and IDs. These formats are not interchangeable. Do not copy a Python manifest into a Rust run, reuse IDs across versions, or mix extractor output images/HTML. Regenerate runs from source PDFs during migration. This is intentional: falsely claiming identical PDF extraction across two engines would undermine provenance.

Both implementations preserve original source text and raster evidence, use the same ownership/quote principles, default to automatic discovery and keep images local. They can produce different text fragments, layout approximations and section boundaries. Golden comparisons should check meaningful text, pages, group ownership and image counts rather than byte-identical HTML or IDs.

8. Segmentation and classification

segment() applies the same conservative policy as Python: explicit known evidence headings and closes define sections when the document uses recognized markers; otherwise each page is a candidate. Known header/footer text is exported separately as furniture. Repeated evidence table rows are retained. Open marked sections can continue to the next page; an interrupted or unclosed section has a boundary note. One PDF is accepted, so the converter's page sequence must be contiguous. Source slice offsets are metadata, not reconstruction of absent evidence.

The source HTML is produced before classification. read_html() then reads it, checks IDs, section ownership, original page, wrapper type and exact text against original Data, and returns compact section/block text with image reference IDs. It does not return styles, base64 images, local paths or browser markup to the LLM.

classify() sends one eligible section per request. It supplies prior discovered names as known_groups, or exact targets in explicit payload mode. The instruction asks for the primary named entity implied by the document; it does not force a server, service or operation category. Start and stop for the same identity belong together. Different similar identifiers must remain separate.

Assigned names must be trimmed, nonempty, at most 200 characters, contain no control characters, and occur at an exact identifier boundary in source text. Fixed-target mode additionally requires membership in the input target list. Source membership prevents invented names; it does not prove that the chosen grouping level is correct.

Status Meaning / target rule
assigned One identified group; non-null valid target
uncertain Missing/ambiguous identity; null target
mixed Inseparable different primary identities; null target
unmatched No supplied target fits; null target

Blank/image-only identity is withheld locally. Explicit fixed-target mode also withholds ambiguous boundaries locally. Discovery asks the model to assess boundary notes conservatively. Image-bearing text sections are not withheld merely because images exist. Mixed sections are never duplicated into multiple process documents. Confidence is an uncalibrated model estimate, not a probability guarantee.

Any invalid identity/decision or systemic provider failure stops routing. Local extraction remains reviewable, and the error is distinct from evidence uncertainty. Sequential discovery is intentional because known_groups depends on previous results. Blindly parallelizing it could create naming inconsistencies, overwhelm provider limits and consume more quota without improving accuracy.

9. HTML export and validation

html_document() filters source pages to owned units, positions them at their original coordinates, escapes text, and embeds normalized PNGs. Unselected evidence leaves blank areas. export() refuses missing, duplicate or foreign section decisions and inconsistent status/target pairs, then writes one document per group plus the review artifacts:

source.html
process-01-<safe-slug>.html
process-02-<safe-slug>.html
withheld.html
furniture.html
index.html
manifest.json
images/<unit-id>.png

The numeric prefix prevents filename collisions when distinct names have the same slug. Names are HTML-escaped and filenames are generated locally. Exported HTML is assembled from original evidence, never from model-generated markup.

read_html() rechecks the selected file before every question. Missing, edited, duplicate, foreign or swapped evidence fails before an API request. Source Data is held in memory for the current run; there is no silent fallback to cached text when HTML fails validation. This is an integrity check against current source provenance, not a cryptographic signature of files edited by an administrator.

10. Process-isolated chat and the abstention correction

Chat stores history by (target, optional chunk). Process selection controls the only HTML read for the question; all-group source HTML and other process histories are never used as chat context. Full scope and /chunk N have separate histories. /clear affects only the current scope.

The user reported a successful classification followed by a generic abstention for a compound start/stop question. A schema-valid abstain: true is not an HTTP failure. The revised CHAT_INSTRUCTION asks the model to answer supported parts of a multipart question and explicitly identify unsupported parts. It distinguishes services within a host group and does not treat state changes at different times as automatically contradictory. These are generic rules, not an answer hardcoded for the reported example. The model can still abstain if evidence is missing or conflicting; the application must not force a fabricated answer to suppress errors.

Per-question steps:

  1. Resolve the selected HTML path inside this run and validate all its evidence.
  2. Apply the explicit chunk restriction, if any. No automatic switch to another process or document is permitted.
  3. Retain at most four conversational turns, and remove oldest turns until history occupies at most one quarter of the budget. The CLI reports history reduction.
  4. Serialize instructions, schema, question, history and evidence using the same message builder that will perform the request. Reserve 1024 characters for bounded correction feedback. The budget counts characters, not tokens.
  5. Pack every original unit into bounded batches. A large section can span batches, but each unit's original section/page/ID remains unchanged. Nothing is silently dropped. If one unit or question cannot fit, fail with /clear/question/budget recovery instructions.
  6. Ask each batch and validate the JSON schema and every quoted reference. A citation must match one supplied text unit, section and original page, with a nonempty exact substring. Image references cannot be quoted as unseen text.
  7. If a citation is invalid, send one bounded semantic repair request using only the same batch. A parent identifier may be resolved only when its exact quote, section and page identify one unique text unit.
  8. If any batch remains invalid, abstain for the entire question. API failures also fail the question rather than presenting partial success. Valid batch answers are labeled, joined and accompanied by deduplicated checked citations.
  9. Save only the validated local result in this process/scope history. Automatically display local images from cited sections where the terminal supports it.

The code does not use another LLM to synthesize multiple batch answers. That avoids introducing an unchecked global conclusion, but means chronology, contradictions or comparisons spanning batches need human review of their cited evidence. A batch may say a fact is not established in its supplied text; that is not a claim about all other batches or all real-world events. For compact evidence fitting in one request, the model sees all selected evidence together.

A valid quote does not prove that every model paraphrase follows logically. There is no theorem prover or independent semantic verifier. The user should inspect quotes for consequential decisions. If the model abstains, its unchecked prose is replaced with local guidance to /evidence and /images instead of displaying possibly invented claims inside an abstention explanation.

11. Endpoint and response compatibility

Client::new() validates the endpoint and modes. URLs must use HTTPS except loopback HTTP, have no userinfo/fragment/obvious secret query key, and no embedded whitespace/control characters. Final request URLs preserve query parameters while adding the route to the path. A standard full /chat/completions URL is preserved; --url-type chat preserves a custom full route. Custom routes have no inferred model catalog. Keys live only in the hidden prompt result and Client; the client intentionally does not implement Debug.

request() enforces consent again, attaches only the chosen auth header, rejects redirects, uses a ten-second connect timeout and a ninety-second total HTTP timeout. It reuses a connection pool. It suppresses raw provider bodies and underlying transport errors. HTTP 401, 403, 404, 429 and 400 have distinct actionable messages; other failures retain the status code. Catalog requests filter unavailable routes.

complete() sends temperature zero, output allowance 2048 and the selected JSON response mode. The prompt explicitly requests a result instance instead of echoing the schema. It accepts strict JSON or one enclosing JSON code fence, rejects arbitrary prose, reads the Chat Completions envelope, detects output truncation, and validates all fields locally. There is at most one JSON/schema correction. Truncation and transport/provider errors are not automatically retried.

validate() implements only the schema vocabulary used by decision_schema() and answer_schema(): exact object fields, arrays, string/null/Boolean/integer/number, enum, and numeric bounds. It is not a general JSON Schema implementation. If new schema constructs are added, extend and test validation or adopt a maintained validator rather than assuming unsupported keywords work. Rust JSON decoding rejects nonfinite JSON numbers. Typed deserialization additionally rejects unknown fields.

Chat semantic citation repair is separate from JSON repair. Worst case per batch: two semantic attempts, each with two structured completions, for four requests. Classification has at most two structured completions per eligible section. More batches increase latency and cost; progress displays actual completed work. The app does not retry a provider outage repeatedly across all sections.

“Any endpoint” here means any appropriately configured compatible URL. It does not implement native Messages, Responses, Gemini or arbitrary HTTP request formats. Use an OpenAI-compatible gateway or implement and test a dedicated adapter. Never solve a compatibility problem by turning off quote checks or TLS verification.

12. Images and terminal rendering

Images remain local during classification, questions and display. image_for_process() first checks that the requested reference belongs to selected sections, canonicalizes its path, and ensures it is an actual file inside this run's images/ directory. A symlink to a file outside that directory fails. It never searches other runs or processes for a missing reference.

image_protocol() checks whether stdout is a terminal, detects Ghostty/Kitty for Kitty graphics or iTerm2/WezTerm for OSC 1337, and avoids unconfigured multiplexers. EVIDENCE_IMAGE_PROTOCOL=kitty|iterm2|none is a deliberate override. Redirection to a file remains plain text even with an override.

image_escape() emits base64-encoded local PNG bytes in protocol-specific framing. Kitty payloads use 4096-byte chunks; OSC 1337 includes byte size and inline display attributes. These are terminal output bytes, never HTTP message content. Unsupported terminals print the absolute path and offer a system viewer for explicit selection. Automatic answer-reference display prints paths without repeatedly prompting.

Source and model text are filtered for terminal control characters before printing; only the renderer deliberately emits control escapes. Tests verify generated escape bytes and local ownership, not that a particular terminal visibly renders them. Validate on the deployment terminal with and without tmux/screen.

13. Troubleshooting and safe reproduction

Symptom Diagnostic and repair
Model abstains after classification Use /evidence to confirm the requested fact is actual text in this group. Ask a supported part. Check service distinctions and original pages. Use /images for facts existing only in screenshots.
Compound question only partly supported Expect a partial cited answer plus a missing-evidence statement, not invented confirmation of both events. Evaluate the model with a controlled fixture.
Unsupported/foreign citations after repair Inspect unit IDs and single-line quote requirements. Test a capable exact model; preserve rejection rules.
Context overflow /clear, shorter question, /chunk N, or increase --context-chars only within the model's capacity. A single massive unit cannot be silently split into invented provenance.
Cannot start converter Install Poppler and verify pdftohtml -v, pdftotext -v, pdftoppm -v on PATH.
Converter failed/timed out Check encrypted/corrupt PDF, disk/output permissions, unusual size/layout; use a sanitized local reproduction. Timeout is 180 seconds in run_tool().
Wrong original pages Confirm _pages_START-END.pdf convention and actual count; otherwise numbering begins at one.
Wrong segmentation Compare source coordinates and headings, retain all units, add a layout-specific regression before changing generic rules.
Wrong group identity Inspect source and decision reasons; review grouping level or use explicit targets. Do not hardcode a sample name.
HTTP 401/403 Key and header mode; router unified key versus upstream provider key; model entitlement.
HTTP 404 Base/full route and exact model ID.
HTTP 400 JSON mode, request parameters, provider context/output limits.
HTTP 429 Quota/rate limit; retry later or select an available model deliberately.
Redirect or TLS failure Correct final trusted URL/certificates/proxy; do not disable security checks.
Invalid result schema Confirm provider emits JSON result instances and supports configured mode. One repair is automatic.
HTML provenance changed Regenerate run from the source; do not replace it with another group's file.
Graphics path only Unsupported terminal/piped stdout/multiplexer; choose compatible terminal or local viewer.
Cargo cannot download/build Confirm toolchain, registry access and lockfile; fetch dependencies before using --offline.
Loopback test permission denied The test sandbox forbids a local listener. Run in an authorized local test environment; no live provider is needed.

For a bug report, collect the package/commit, rustc --version, Poppler version, OS/architecture, safe error, selected group/question and a minimal sanitized PDF. Use fake keys in reproductions. Do not log Client, request headers, raw provider bodies or production evidence to shared CI logs. Exact local file errors are useful for operators; do not expose filesystem paths in a public service without review.

14. Build, tests, release and rollback

From the repository root:

cargo fmt --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --check
cargo clippy --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings
cargo test --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --locked
cargo build --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --release --locked
cargo doc --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --no-deps --locked

--offline may be appended after all locked dependencies have been fetched. The first fetch requires network access to package registries. The application tests never contact an external model. One test binds a loopback mock HTTP server with dummy credentials to exercise actual URL construction, headers and error handling; others inject the Model trait oracle. Real PDF extraction tests require Poppler. Do not silently skip these tests and report conversion verified.

src/tests.rs generates temporary PDF bytes directly in Rust, including a real raster image XObject, real text and PDF cross-reference offsets. Tests do not require a Python fixture generator. They verify extraction/conservation, grouping of arbitrary similar names, image membership, HTML escaping/read-back, offsets, segmentation/continuation, strict schema validation, invalid identities, tampering, foreign citations, batches/history, partial supported answers and error boundaries. A good mock answer proves application handling, not a real model's accuracy.

Before release: run both Python and Rust suites, compare representative original PDFs in both outputs, validate an approved synthetic live-provider example, check native image display on the target terminal, and verify output-directory permissions. Record exact dependency/compiler/Poppler versions and release artifact hashes. Build separately for each target platform and package/document Poppler installation. Keep keys out of the artifact and out of deployment command arguments.

Rollback is selecting the prior binary or Python environment and creating a fresh run from the original PDF. Existing run documents remain independent files. Do not pretend a rollback can reuse different-extractor provenance IDs. This work does not deploy a service, commit changes, or push a release automatically.

15. Performance: what is optimized and what to measure

Use --release for measurements. Debug-build timings are not representative. Rust removes interpreter startup and Python object overhead in the application logic. Poppler performs bounded parallel native PDF conversion, existing PNG bytes are reused, and the HTTP client reuses connections. There is no claim that Rust can make an upstream model generate tokens faster.

The CLI reports conversion time (including source HTML), routing/final-export time, and total processing time after configuration. These exclude the user's prompt interaction and preview. Classification latency includes sequential API waiting; chat batching can add multiple API calls. A text model's context, prompt quality, provider throughput and rate limits commonly dominate end-to-end latency.

For a fair local comparison:

  1. Use the same source bytes and output filesystem.
  2. Disable preview and all network calls; export source and unclassified evidence in both implementations. Include or exclude process startup consistently.
  3. Use a release Rust binary and the pinned Python environment.
  4. Repeat several times with fresh output directories; report median and sample range, page/image/unit counts, hardware and versions.
  5. Review output quality and segmentation before treating speed as success.
  6. Measure provider-dependent runs separately using an approved controlled input.

The two extractors can emit different fragment counts and styles. A timing comparison is therefore a workflow comparison, not proof that equivalent internal operations are faster by a fixed ratio. Large images, image normalization, output size and disk can dominate local time. Never replace a measured slowdown with an “ultra fast” claim.

Measured local result — 23 October 2026

Environment: macOS 27.0.1 on arm64; Rust/Cargo 1.95.0 release build, Poppler 26.08.0, repository Python 3.13 environment. Three fresh-output runs per implementation/input, sequentially on the same machine. Measurements include process startup, conversion, source HTML and unclassified review exports; exclude preview, user interaction and all HTTP calls. These are small local samples, not a production load test or a promise about another machine.

Input Python median (range) Rust median (range), four conversion workers
10 pages 1.146 s (1.043–1.177) 0.565 s (0.564–1.005)
52 pages 3.580 s (3.452–3.586) 2.155 s (2.139–2.163)

Both implementations produced 20 sections/20 images/441 text units for the 10-page input and 102 sections/102 images/2248 text units for the combined 52-page input. The combined PDF was assembled locally from the six existing slices for this test; the source slices were untouched. Counts establish extraction parity on this input, not correct model classification or universal layout equivalence.

The first serial Poppler implementation was slower than Python (about 7.99 s versus 3.45 s for 52 pages). Profiling isolated native image conversion as the bottleneck. The final version reuses PNG bytes and converts independent page ranges concurrently before ordered segmentation. This measured optimization, not Rust syntax alone, explains the improvement. --pdf-workers 1 trades throughput for lower concurrent CPU/memory use. Provider latency remains separately unmeasured.

Historical verification record — prior snapshot

  • Python reference: 44 tests passed.
  • Rust: 17 tests passed, including real Poppler PDFs and a dummy loopback HTTP API.
  • cargo fmt --check, Clippy with warnings denied, release build, and generated API documentation were checked successfully.
  • Serial/parallel equivalence includes a section continuing across worker ranges.
  • Actual graphics rendering and a live external model have not been verified. Tests check protocol bytes, provenance, and application behavior using controlled responses.

16. Extending and managing the code

For a new provider protocol, implement Model with the same text-only/citation contract. Add adapter-specific URL/auth/schema tests. Do not broaden Client into an arbitrary endpoint executor accepting unvalidated parameters.

For layout changes, start with a generated PDF demonstrating the problem. Assert all source units appear exactly once in sections/furniture, image ownership remains correct, and new boundaries do not merge independent processes. Mixed layouts without separable evidence remain withheld until a justified rule exists.

For chat improvements, verify exact serialized request bodies, scope/history isolation, all-unit coverage, correction limits and partial-answer behavior. A future cross-batch synthesis step must account for conflicts and validate output against original evidence; joining model summaries alone is not enough.

For local OCR, preserve image-region/page provenance and clearly distinguish OCR text from original text. That is a future feature, not something the current model can infer by seeing an image reference ID.

For a resumable/server deployment, design authentication, workspace ownership, manifest integrity, concurrency, retention and publication semantics first. This program is currently a local per-run CLI. Do not expose its output directory as a public web root or launch an unrestricted PDF execution service as an incidental extension of this rewrite.

For dependency upgrades, keep Cargo.lock, review release notes and re-run extraction/golden-boundary checks. cargo update without a specific intent may change many transitive dependencies. Upgrade deliberately and record measurable behavior changes. Check applicable dependencies/native-tool redistribution terms when packaging releases for an organization.

17. Reference documentation

Implementation references, accessed during this handover:

  • quick-xml 0.41: event parsing and XML text/attribute decoding.
  • reqwest 0.12.28 blocking client: timeouts, TLS and redirect policy.
  • Poppler project: native PDF utilities; use the installed pdftohtml -h for its exact supported flags.
  • Python handover: original architecture, reported abstention investigation, and baseline operations.

The source code, locked dependency versions, installed tool help and executable regressions are the authoritative implementation contract. Historical test counts or examples in the original handoff are not evidence of live-provider correctness.

04 / Optimizations

Optimization decisions and remaining work

The final pass changes local CPU/allocation/I/O work without broadening model access. This pass has not been executed or benchmarked, following the owner's instruction. The earlier timing table in the Rust guide belongs to the prior parallel-conversion snapshot, not these final edits.

Implemented changes

Component Change Reason Preserved behavior
Rust PDF hashing Read SHA-256 through a reusable 64 KiB buffer Avoid allocating a second whole-PDF byte array just for hashing Same source digest and deterministic IDs
Rust HTML escaping One traversal into one String buffer Avoid five full string replacement passes Same escaping of ampersand, brackets and quotes; Unicode retained
Rust converter image paths Resolve worker scratch root once, lazily Avoid redundant root filesystem canonicalization for each image Every image is individually canonicalized and containment-checked
Rust image output Copy existing PNG bytes; normalize other supported formats Avoid decode/re-encode of already suitable evidence images Local PNG references and image ownership
Rust PDF conversion Up to four independent page-range workers Overlap native CPU work on multi-core machines Ordered merge before segmentation, cross-range continuations
Rust label collection Set-assisted membership with ordered output list Avoid repeated growing-vector membership scans First-appearance order and exact identity strings
Rust export validation Construct decision schema once for the export Avoid reconstructing the same trusted schema per section Every decision still validated
Rust CSS color validation Fixed ASCII length/prefix/hex check Avoid a regex for a tiny fixed-format field Only six-digit hex colors accepted; fallback black
Both chat packers Append in place, undo only the overflowing unit Avoid cloning all already-packed sections on each line Exact request-budget checks, every unit retained in order
Python completion validator Reuse one Draft 2020-12 validator across bounded attempts Avoid repeated schema meta-validation inside each completion Result types/fields still checked after every response
Python CLI argument validation Reject invalid context budget before setup Avoid preview/configuration work on an invalid request Same minimum accepted budget and error text
Rust HTTP Reusable client connection pool Avoid unnecessary connection setup between calls TLS, redirects, consent, auth and timeouts retained
Chat history Four-turn and budget bounds Prevent unbounded conversational growth Per-process/per-chunk isolation and reduction notice

Only PDF hashing has bounded 64 KiB auxiliary buffering. The application still holds page/section records, converted XML, HTML strings and some duplicated source structures in memory. The packers still serialize candidate payloads to measure exact character budgets; removing clones does not make batching generally linear. No measured gain is claimed for the final allocation edits.

Optimizations deliberately deferred

Parallel discovery: each request receives previous known groups. Running all requests independently can produce inconsistent names. Keep discovery sequential unless an explicit reconciliation design and accuracy evaluation justify a change. Fixed-target routing could use bounded concurrency later, with provider limits and failure cancellation handled deliberately.

Fuzzy citation matching: changing an exact quote check to approximate matching would weaken evidence validation. Do not trade correctness for fewer abstentions. Repair a model response or use better evidence instead.
