Caching selected HTML forever: every question must notice file changes. A future cache needs a defined integrity policy and reliable invalidation. A cache of prior model answers also needs document/model/prompt/scope keys and does not guarantee that a repeated question is equivalent.

Approximate budget arithmetic: token estimation may improve packing efficiency, but silent overflow or dropping evidence is unacceptable. Any estimator must still use a final exact serialized-message check and preserve every unit.

Removing the native PDF converter: a pure Rust replacement would need robust fonts, coordinates, transformations, masks and image decoding. Do not replace a PDF engine with string searches to save a subprocess. Poppler is an explicit runtime dependency and is replaceable only through a well-defined extractor contract.

Cross-batch synthesis: a second model call could make answers more fluent, but it can invent a global conclusion or hide conflicts. Add one only with validation against original quotes and clear coverage semantics. Current batch answers remain explicitly labeled so a user sees the limits.

Async service infrastructure: this is a local per-user CLI. Adding a web server, database, vector database, queue or agent framework is unnecessary for the current workflow and requires separate access-control and lifecycle decisions.

If performance work resumes later

Measure separately: process startup, PDF metadata/conversion, image processing, HTML export/read-back, context packing, HTTP wait, and terminal display. Preserve source bytes and record page/section/image counts. Compare equivalent fresh runs on the same hardware. A faster extraction run does not establish routing accuracy, reduced provider latency or correct native terminal rendering.

The user explicitly excluded running checks during this final pass. Commands in the guides are instructions for a future maintainer, not actions performed now.

05 / Restore source

Restore the exact application source

This HTML includes the complete application source and dependency lockfile. The source explorer below provides per-file copy/download and the full JSON bundle. To restore without asking an AI to regenerate code, save the script below as rebuild_evidence.py, then use a new destination directory:

python3 rebuild_evidence.py /path/to/index.html /path/to/new-project

The script uses only the Python standard library, verifies every embedded file hash, refuses existing destinations, writes the exact files, and copies this guide to new-project/rust/index.html. It does not install dependencies, run applications, call APIs or perform Git operations. Python is needed only for this optional restoration script; the Rust app itself does not require Python.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Restore the exact embedded source snapshot into a NEW directory; no app is run."""
import hashlib
import json
import shutil
import sys
from html.parser import HTMLParser
from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath

class BundleParser(HTMLParser):
    """Read only the inert source-bundle script, never execute HTML or JavaScript."""
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(convert_charrefs=False)
        self.active = False
        self.parts = []
        self.count = 0

    def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
        if tag == 'script' and dict(attrs).get('id') == 'source-bundle':
            self.count += 1
            self.active = True

    def handle_data(self, data):
        if self.active:
            self.parts.append(data)

    def handle_endtag(self, tag):
        if tag == 'script':
            self.active = False

def main():
    """Validate all hashes/relative paths first; refuse to overwrite existing work."""
    if len(sys.argv) != 3:
        raise SystemExit('Usage: python3 rebuild_evidence.py PATH/TO/index.html NEW_DIRECTORY')
    document = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve(strict=True)
    destination = Path(sys.argv[2]).absolute()
    if destination.exists() or destination.is_symlink():
        raise SystemExit('Choose a new directory; existing files are never overwritten.')
    parser = BundleParser()
    parser.feed(document.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    if parser.count != 1:
        raise SystemExit('Expected exactly one embedded source bundle.')
    bundle = json.loads(''.join(parser.parts))
    if bundle.get('format') != 'evidence-splitter-source-v1':
        raise SystemExit('Unrecognized source bundle format.')
    seen, files = set(), []
    for item in bundle['files']:
        name = item['path']
        relative = PurePosixPath(name)
        if (not name or chr(92) in name or ':' in name or relative.is_absolute()
                or any(part in ('', '.', '..') for part in name.split('/')) or name in seen):
            raise SystemExit('Invalid or duplicate source path in bundle.')
        seen.add(name)
        content = item['content'].encode('utf-8')
        if hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest() != item['sha256']:
            raise SystemExit('Source hash mismatch: ' + name)
        files.append((relative, content))
    if not files:
        raise SystemExit('Source bundle is empty.')
    destination.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=False)
    for relative, content in files:
        target = destination.joinpath(*relative.parts)
        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        target.write_bytes(content)
    documentation = destination / 'rust' / 'index.html'
    documentation.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    shutil.copyfile(document, documentation)
    print(f'Restored {len(files)} source/configuration files and documentation into {destination}')
    print('No dependencies installed, application executed, API called, or Git operation performed.')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

Hashes detect accidental drift inside the bundle; they do not authenticate the publisher. Future maintainers should update source snapshots when code changes. The historical original handoff and user PDFs are not required runtime files and are not embedded. The source bundle includes the Python handover Markdown because it is also published independently with the Python fixes.

File paths are relative to the project root. The snapshot includes both implementations, existing regression files and the Rust lockfile. No API keys, user PDFs, run outputs or binaries are included.

# Evidence Splitter

Detailed Python engineering handover: [PYTHON_MAINTENANCE.md](PYTHON_MAINTENANCE.md).

A small Python terminal application that converts PDF evidence into positioned HTML,
asks a text model to discover and group process/entity evidence automatically, and provides
process-isolated Q&A with checked citations from the selected process HTML file. Images remain local throughout model calls.

The original brief and detailed requirements remain unchanged in [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md).
The application accepts one user-supplied PDF. No web server, database, vector store,
or agent framework is required.

## Run

Python 3.10 or newer is required. Create a virtual environment and install dependencies:

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```

Launch the terminal workflow:

```sh
.venv/bin/python cli.py
```

At the prompt, drag and drop one PDF into the terminal, or type its full path and
press Enter. Paths with spaces may be typed literally, quoted, or shell-escaped.
Directory input is rejected, and there is no preset document location.

A local preview shows the filename, page count, file size and up to 800 characters
of verbatim first-page text extracted locally by PyMuPDF. This is an excerpt, not
an AI-generated summary. Kitty/iTerm2 terminals also show a first-page thumbnail; other
terminals offer to open the original PDF in the system viewer. Previewing makes no
network requests and happens before credentials are collected.

Enter your API URL, enter the API key at the hidden prompt, and enter a model ID.
For routers with automatic selection, press Enter at the model prompt. `/models`
can list available model IDs. Approve sending document text to that service.

The LLM then discovers the groups automatically. **You do not need to type process
names or supply a target payload.** The LLM reads section headings and identifying
fields to infer which named process/entity each piece of evidence belongs to. The
code contains no predefined process names or fixed preference for server versus
service names. Repeated evidence for the same identity is collected across pages;
start, stop and verification records stay together. Similar or unrelated identities
remain separate. Discovered names must occur exactly in the source evidence, and
previously discovered names are provided to keep naming consistent across sections.

After processing, the terminal lists every created group and its HTML path. Select
a number or group name to chat. `/ls` lists the generated files again. The model
receives only the selected group's HTML evidence and its own conversation history.
Reference images attached to cited sections are displayed locally after answers.

Normal API defaults are Bearer authentication, automatic URL interpretation and
`json_object` responses. For FreeLLMAPI, enter the router's **unified key**; the default
Bearer header is appropriate. These advanced flags are available if your API needs them:

```sh
python cli.py --auth api-key --response-format json_schema --url-type chat
```

Any hosted HTTPS base URL or full OpenAI-compatible chat-completion URL is accepted;
HTTP is also supported for loopback routers. No `/v1` suffix is forced. `--url-type chat`
uses a custom full URL directly, and query parameters such as `api-version` are preserved.
A provider using a different native JSON protocol needs a compatible gateway or adapter.
`auto` as a model name requires router support; direct providers need an exact model ID.

For an explicit fixed list of service/process targets instead of discovery, supply
`--payload targets.example.json`. This optional mode preserves exact supplied labels
and keeps different services separate even if they share a server. It is not part
of the default interactive workflow.

To convert and inspect everything without an API key or any network calls:

```sh
.venv/bin/python cli.py --pdf "/path/to/evidence.pdf" --extract-only
```

Add `--no-browser` to exit after writing the files. Here “browser” means the terminal
document menu; it does not automatically launch a web browser. Local-only exports
mark all evidence as unclassified; no discovered process files are created without a model.

Each run creates `runs/<timestamp>/`:

| Output | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `source.html` | Full PDF converted to positioned HTML before classification |
| `index.html` | Links to every target and review document, plus all decision reasons |
| `process-01-group-name.html`, etc. | Independent HTML containing only assigned source evidence |
| `withheld.html` | Uncertain, mixed, unmatched or not-yet-classified evidence |
| `furniture.html` | Recognized document headers/footers, retained for audit |
| `manifest.json` | Source hashes, original pages, positions, section/block/line IDs and decisions |
| `images/` | Actual extracted PNG files with provenance IDs as filenames |

HTML embeds local images at export time so each document can be copied independently.
Outputs contain source evidence; treat them with the same access controls as the PDF.
Keys, endpoint configuration and chat histories are not written to disk.

## Terminal commands

Select any numbered process document, or `w` for withheld evidence. The menu also
provides the results table and index. Inside a document:

| Command | Action |
| --- | --- |
| `/ls` | List every process HTML file, label, size and assigned-section count |
| `/evidence` | Show source text, section chunk numbers, line/block IDs and original pages |
| `/images` or `/images 1` | List numbered local references or immediately show one |
| `/html` | Open this document in the system browser |
| `/chunk N` | Explicitly limit chat to one section, with a separate history |
| `/all` | Restore full-process chat scope |
| `/clear` | Clear the current process/scope conversation history |
| `/process` or `/back` | Return to the document selector |
| `/help`, `/quit` | Show help or exit |

Enter a question to chat. The selected process HTML is read from disk on each question, and only its extracted
evidence text and that process's own history are sent. Missing or modified HTML
fails locally; there is no fallback to another document or in-memory evidence. Withheld evidence is inspectable but cannot be used as process chat evidence.
A response must cite an existing text line ID, the correct section and original page,
and an exact nonempty source quote. Foreign or fabricated citations cause abstention.
**Citation validity does not prove every paraphrase is accurate.** Inspect the quoted
text and original evidence when correctness matters.

Ghostty and Kitty use native Kitty graphics; iTerm2 and WezTerm use inline image
escapes. `/images 1` shows the first image in the selected group immediately, or
say “show me the reference image” to choose one. Answer citations also display their
associated reference images automatically. All image resolution is local and scoped
to the selected group; image bytes never enter LLM requests.

Plain terminals cannot display actual inline images. In that case the CLI gives the
local image path and offers the system viewer when selecting `/images`. Use a compatible
terminal for native display. Multiplexers may block graphics; run directly in the terminal,
or set `EVIDENCE_IMAGE_PROTOCOL=kitty` or `iterm2` only if your setup supports passthrough.
Protocol output is covered by tests; visual rendering requires a compatible terminal.

Ctrl-C cancels the workflow. Conversion is exported before classification begins, so
a provider failure or cancellation leaves a local review document. No partial routing
is published as a successful run. Fix the endpoint/model/quota problem and rerun.

## How the code is organized

There are only two application modules and one test module. Every function has a
purpose docstring; comments explain the main boundaries and security decisions.

- `pipeline.py`: extraction, segmentation, text-only HTTP client, strict JSON/schema
  checks, classification, HTML export, local image resolution and cited chat.
- `cli.py`: prompts, progress, results, document menus, isolated chats and local viewers.
- `test_pipeline.py`: disposable controlled PDFs, mocked HTTP responses and regression tests.
- `requirements.txt` and `targets.example.json`: dependencies and the input payload format.

The backend uses PyMuPDF DICT extraction, retaining line/span coordinates and parent
block IDs. The flow is **PDF → source.html → text extracted from source.html → LLM
classification → independent process HTML files → chat using only the selected HTML**.
The model decides routing; the backend assembles the original HTML evidence according
to those decisions. The model does not rewrite the evidence. HTML is parsed locally
into compact section/block text; markup and base64 images are excluded from requests.
Source IDs and exact text are validated before classification or chat. IDs use
a source-file hash plus original page/block/line numbers. IDs are deterministic for the
same file; rewriting or re-slicing a PDF changes its hash and therefore its IDs.
Filenames like `Synthetic_Server_Data_pages_11-20.pdf` preserve source offsets.
Sections continue across contiguous pages within the selected PDF. A page slice
can be supplied as a single PDF, but missing context remains quarantined.

Segmentation recognizes explicit evidence headings and close markers when present.
Line-level IDs handle headings that share a PDF block with introductory prose. Standalone
large `Start`/`Stop` headings are also recognized. Without these markers, each page
is a candidate section. Discovery sends boundary notes to the model and requires it
to withhold ambiguous/mixed identity; missing-page continuations remain quarantined.
Optional fixed-target mode retains strict boundary quarantine. The furniture allowlist is explicit;
there is no generic “remove repeated bottom rows” rule. Every extracted text/image
unit belongs to a section or the auditable furniture collection.

Classification uses one request per eligible section. A section's associated images
stay attached when the process is clear from text. Mixed evidence is withheld, never
duplicated across targets. There is no confidence cutoff: displayed confidence is a
model estimate, not a measured accuracy score. Routing depends on the model and is
not claimed to be ground truth.

Requests reject authenticated redirects and non-HTTPS remote endpoints, use bounded
timeouts, and validate structured results locally. Invalid JSON/schema gets one
correction attempt; invalid identities and provider errors stop the run. No raw provider
error bodies or tracebacks are printed. A 24,000-character serialized message budget
bounds context conservatively; it is **not a tokenizer or a guarantee for every model**.
Classification overflow fails without truncation. Chat visits all selected evidence in
bounded batches when needed, preserving original IDs. Answers are explicitly scoped
and quoted; they are not synthesized into a global conclusion. Conversation history
is bounded to four recent turns and a quarter of the context budget, with a notice
when history is reduced. No source evidence is silently discarded.

Use `--context-chars N` to configure the request-character budget for your model.
An oversized individual line/question fails clearly: use `/clear`, shorten the
question, select `/chunk N`, or increase the budget within model limits.
Questions with several parts can receive a cited answer for the supported parts
while explicitly identifying missing evidence. One missing event does not suppress
a separately documented event. Citation errors get one bounded correction; foreign
or fabricated references still cause abstention.

The final allocation optimizations were not executed or retested, as requested.
Earlier offline verification is recorded in the Python handover.

## Limits and design choices

Ordinary unmarked pages are evaluated by the model for identity. Pages containing
inseparable evidence for multiple groups are withheld instead of duplicated. New
complex layouts may benefit from additional section boundary rules in `pipeline.py`;
interleaved columns and ambiguous continuations still require review.

Text spans and raster image positions are retained, with browser font fallbacks.
Vector drawings, embedded fonts, rotated text and complex PDF transformations are not
reproduced exactly. Scanned image text is not OCR'd or sent to a vision model. Image-only
identity is therefore withheld. An image overlapping independent sections cannot be
semantically subdivided by this baseline. Always verify unfamiliar layouts against the
source PDF before using their classification downstream.

The preserved 52-page dataset was used for the initial offline verification. The
application has no dependency on that dataset or its local directory. The test
generator creates a smaller known-label fixture rather than a new 30–40-page mock. Automatic group discovery is the default; a web UI is outside this baseline.

Implementation references: [PyMuPDF extraction documentation](https://pymupdf.readthedocs.io/en/latest/app1.html)
explains the positioned text/image representation and HTML layout caveats;
[FreeLLMAPI REST reference](https://github.com/tashfeenahmed/freellmapi/blob/main/docs/en/api/01-rest-api.md)
documents unified authentication, model catalog availability and explicit `auto` routing.
The [Groq compatibility guide](https://console.groq.com/docs/openai) describes its
OpenAI-compatible base path; [Azure REST authentication and versioning](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/reference#authentication)
documents Bearer/`api-key` authentication and `api-version` query parameters.
Model IDs and endpoint availability are runtime configuration, not hardcoded assumptions.

## Historical tests and verification report — before final optimization edits

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m unittest -v test_pipeline
```

Generate the controlled three-page fixture if you want to inspect it manually:

```sh
.venv/bin/python test_pipeline.py --make-fixture /tmp/evidence-fixture.pdf
```

Its exact targets are `alpha-api` and `beta-api`. It includes a mixed-process page,
a cross-page continuation, repeated table rows, images, mixed and unmatched sections,
and orphan/unknown evidence. Tests generate it in temporary directories automatically.

- Offline regression suite: **44 tests passed on the earlier snapshot**. Tests exercise
  real PDF extraction/export and mock only HTTP responses. Request bodies are inspected
  for text-only content, selected-process isolation and absence of image data/paths.
- Automatic grouping: arbitrary process names across different page counts
  produce separate groups with all original images. CLI tests prove there is no manual
  target prompt; invented/partial labels fail validation. All responses are mocked.
- Native image protocols: Ghostty/Kitty and iTerm2/WezTerm escape output, numbered
  references and isolation are tested without network calls.
- HTML grounding: classification reads the source HTML, chat reads only the selected
  process HTML, and missing/edited/swapped documents are rejected before HTTP.
- Hosted API configuration: base paths, full/custom chat URLs, trailing slashes,
  preserved query parameters, catalog URL construction and both authentication
  headers are verified with mocked HTTP. Embedded URL credentials are rejected.
- Single-PDF input: typed, quoted and shell-escaped paths with spaces are tested.
  Directories and missing files are rejected; preview extracts real PDF metadata/text
  locally and reports corrupt PDFs before model configuration.
- Preserved dataset (initial directory-based verification before single-PDF input): **52 pages, 102 logical sections, 102 extracted images** verified
  locally. The first 100 sections have explicit boundaries. The final two lack close
  markers; discovery now asks the model to assess their available text and boundary notes. Counts are not classification ground truth.
- Error coverage includes invalid JSON/schema, wrong IDs, inconsistent assignments,
  missing/duplicate decisions, provider/auth/quota errors, redirects, context overflow,
  foreign images/citations, fabricated quotes and incorrect source pages.
- Interactive PTY smoke checks: hidden key input did not echo the dummy key; consent
  refusal stopped before network access. A mocked classification run showed real page/section
  progress and accessible process menus. Local image fallback, process switching and
  cancellation were also checked. Actual on-screen graphics rendering and a live-provider waiting
  spinner were not visually verified.
- Live provider classification: **not run**. No document text was disclosed during
  development. Real routing quality and provider-specific structured output compatibility
  remain to be verified after the user's explicit consent in the CLI.

# Python implementation: engineering and internship handover

Updated 23 October 2026. Read this alongside `pipeline.py`, `cli.py`, and
`test_pipeline.py`. This document describes the implementation, including the chat
changes made during this handover. `HANDOFF.md` is historical context: later user
requirements supersede its fixed-target default and its earlier exclusion of Rust.
The Python implementation is retained as an independently runnable reference.

## Current delivery status

The final code changes were not built, tested or benchmarked, following the user's
instruction. Earlier test results describe the preceding snapshot, not the final
allocation optimization edits. Python chat packing now extends batches in place,
rolling back only the overflowing unit, while retaining exact budget checks. Each
completion reuses a Draft 2020-12 schema validator across its bounded attempts.
Invalid context-budget flags are rejected before preview/provider configuration.
Rust implementation and its consolidated HTML handover are maintained separately
under `rust/`; publication of this Python handover does not publish that directory.

## 1. Purpose, current contract, and boundaries

The application turns one PDF into HTML, asks a configured text LLM to classify
logical evidence sections, exports one independent HTML per discovered group,
and answers questions using only the selected group's exported evidence.

The default interaction is: PDF path → local preview → API configuration and
consent → conversion → automatic grouping → process list → questions/images.
Users do not need to name processes in advance. `--payload` is an optional
restriction to supplied names. Names and grouping levels come from document text;
there are no hardcoded production process names. The server/service distinction
matters: a model may choose a host as the primary entity, and that group can then
contain multiple services. Review this interpretation before downstream use.

Images are evidence for human inspection. No image pixels, data URLs, attachments,
or image paths go to the LLM. A text-only model cannot read commands or states that
exist solely inside screenshots. There is no OCR. Native terminal image display
does not change the model's capabilities.

The code validates identities, ownership, exact quotes and pages. It cannot prove
that a classification or a paraphrase is semantically correct. Do not describe the
system as having zero hallucinations or use model confidence as measured accuracy.

## 2. Setup from a clean machine

Requirements: Python 3.10+, a terminal, and installed dependencies in
`requirements.txt`. The code has been tested with the repository's Python 3.13
virtual environment; other declared Python versions need their own compatibility
checks. Network access is needed to install packages and for an approved model
connection, but not for extraction or image viewing.

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.venv/bin/python cli.py
```

Windows activation/launch equivalent:

```powershell
py -m venv .venv
.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.venv\Scripts\python.exe cli.py
```

Run commands from the repository root. Output goes to `runs/` relative to the
working directory. No user's Desktop, home directory, provider URL, or model ID is
a required deployment path. PDF paths may be typed literally, quoted, or pasted
using terminal drag/drop escaping. A directory is not an input PDF.

First perform a local-only conversion:

```sh
.venv/bin/python cli.py --pdf '/path/to/evidence.pdf' --extract-only --no-browser
```

`--no-browser` exits after exports instead of entering the interactive document
menu; it does not disable the earlier local preview. Review `source.html`,
`withheld.html`, `furniture.html`, and `index.html` in the printed run directory.
Use ordinary filesystem access controls for these files: they contain the PDF's
text and images. Nothing encrypts them automatically.

## 3. Configuration and provider onboarding

| Setting | Default and meaning |
| --- | --- |
| PDF | Prompt or `--pdf`; one existing PDF |
| API URL | Prompt; HTTPS base URL or full compatible chat URL |
| Key | Hidden terminal prompt; retained in memory, never intentionally persisted |
| Model | Prompt; exact provider ID or explicit router `auto` |
| `--auth` | `bearer`; alternatively `api-key` header |
| `--response-format` | `json_object`; alternatively strict `json_schema` |
| `--url-type` | `auto`; `base` appends chat route, `chat` preserves full custom URL |
| `--context-chars` | 24000 serialized message characters; minimum CLI value 4000 |
| `--payload` | Optional JSON file with at least two distinct target strings |
| `--extract-only` | No model configuration or requests |
| `EVIDENCE_IMAGE_PROTOCOL` | Optional `kitty`, `iterm2`, or `none` override |

The character budget is not a token budget. Different languages, escaping, and
models have different tokenization. Set it conservatively below the selected
model's context capacity, allowing space for output. Increasing it does not grant
the model a larger context window. The output allowance is 2048 tokens per call.

Base URLs append `/chat/completions` without adding `/v1` implicitly. A URL already
ending in `/chat/completions` is preserved. Query parameters remain after the path,
including API version parameters. For a custom complete route, use `--url-type
chat`. The catalog route is `/models` under the same base; a custom nonstandard
chat URL cannot imply a model catalog, so supply an exact model ID.

The implemented wire protocol is OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions. A generic URL
field does not implement native Anthropic Messages, Gemini generateContent,
OpenAI Responses, or arbitrary proprietary protocols. Such APIs need a compatible
gateway or a deliberately tested adapter. HTTPS can be remote; HTTP is accepted
only for loopback addresses. Redirects, URL credentials, common secret query names,
fragments, malformed ports and unsafe URL whitespace are rejected.

`/models` filters routes marked `needsKey`, `exhausted`, or equivalent execution
status. Availability may still change after listing. `auto` only works if the
configured service supports it. For a forwarding router, the key may be the
router's unified key rather than its upstream provider's key. Never put real keys
in shell arguments, examples, test fixtures, manifests, bug reports, or commits.

## 4. Files and ownership

| File | Responsibility / likely maintainer changes |
| --- | --- |
| `pipeline.py` | Extraction, segmentation, HTML provenance, HTTP, classification, exports, chat |
| `cli.py` | Input, local preview, progress, menus, terminal images and viewer launch |
| `test_pipeline.py` | Real temporary PDF fixtures and mocked HTTP regressions |
| `requirements.txt` | Reproducible pinned Python dependencies |
| `targets.example.json` | Optional fixed-label payload format |
| `README.md` | Short user entry point |
| `HANDOFF.md` | Original brief and historical decisions |
| `PYTHON_MAINTENANCE.md` | This technical operations guide |
| `rust/` | Independent native implementation; see its own handover |

There is no web server, database, scheduler, queue, vector store, model training,
or background ingestion process. A run is local files plus in-memory application
state. A second user should run their own process and output directory. Importing
a manifest and resuming a prior Python run is not currently a CLI feature.

## 5. End-to-end execution and failure boundaries

1. `main()` parses flags; `select_pdf()` normalizes and validates the path.
2. `preview_pdf()` reads the first page locally. The shown excerpt is verbatim
   extraction, not an AI summary. Graphics-capable terminals get a local thumbnail;
   other interactive terminals offer the system viewer.
3. `configure()` gathers endpoint, key and model, then asks for explicit consent.
   No evidence request precedes that approval. Catalog requests also require it.
4. `extract()` creates the run and image directory, validates PDF/page metadata,
   hashes the file, extracts positioned units, calls `segment()`, and writes
   `source.html` before classification starts.
5. `export(..., state='not classified')` creates a reviewable local baseline.
   All sections are withheld; this is not successful routing.
6. `classify()` reads and validates `source.html`, routes eligible sections one at
   a time, validates each result, and reports real completed section counts.
7. Only complete valid decisions are passed to `export(..., state='complete')`.
   It assembles source evidence into independent documents; the model never writes
   or rewrites the HTML evidence.
8. `browse()` lists processes, withheld evidence, index and decisions. A question
   invokes `Chat.ask()` for the selected document. Images are resolved locally.

A provider failure stops classification rather than producing a stream of false
uncertain labels. The previous local baseline remains inspectable. A filesystem
failure during export can leave partial files: exports are not transactional.
Check `manifest.json` state and all referenced files; do not infer completeness
from a directory existing. New runs use new timestamp directories, avoiding reuse
of old successful output as if it belonged to a failed run.

## 6. Data model and provenance invariants

`data` contains `sources`, `pages`, `sections`, and `furniture`. The manifest adds
`state`, `targets`, `documents`, and `decisions`.

- Source: filename, full SHA-256, starting original page, count, and family name.
- Page: deterministic ID, original page number, source/family, width/height, units.
- Text unit: ID, parent `block_id`, page/page ID/source, bounding box, text, spans.
- Span: original text, rectangle, font size, flags and color.
- Image unit: analogous provenance plus relative PNG path, no extracted text.
- Section: ID based on its first unit, ordered units, and optional boundary note.
- Decision: `section_id`, `status`, nullable `target`, confidence in [0,1], reason.

Example ID shape: `d<12 hex hash>-p0007-b003-l001`. `b003` is a PDF block and
`l001` identifies a text line within it. A section adds `s-`. Image IDs generally
end at the parent block. A file rewrite changes the hash even if its appearance
is similar; IDs are not durable business identifiers across independently saved
PDF versions. A `_pages_11-20.pdf` filename supplies original page offset 11 and
must contain exactly ten pages. Without that convention, page numbering starts
at one. The application cannot reconstruct omitted pages from a slice.

Every extracted unit must be owned by exactly one section or furniture. Furniture
is an explicit allowlist of known headers/footers; repeated table rows are evidence
and must not be dropped by a generic repetition heuristic. Source image paths
must resolve inside this run's image directory before local display.

HTML wrappers carry `id`, `data-section`, `data-block`, and `data-page`. Text is
escaped; images are embedded as PNG data URLs for standalone copying. `read_html()`
parses known wrappers and checks exact text, page and ownership against in-memory
source provenance. It rejects missing, duplicate, foreign or modified evidence.
Titles/CSS/image bytes are not context. This detects evidence mismatches, not
malicious modification of the entire source-and-manifest trust base. Never treat
arbitrary downloaded manifests as authenticated evidence.

## 7. Segmentation and classification policy

`is_heading()` recognizes explicit evidence headings and sufficiently large
standalone Start/Stop headings. `segment()` carries open sections across contiguous
pages and finishes at explicit close markers. Orphans, interrupted sections,
missing pages and missing close markers receive boundary notes. If the document
has no recognized heading anywhere, each page is an independent candidate.

Discovery sends ordinary boundary notes to the model for conservative assessment;
missing/unrelated continuation pages remain locally withheld. Optional fixed-label
mode withholds every ambiguous boundary before calling the model. A mixed page
with separable recognized sections can be routed independently. An unmarked page
containing inseparable different identities must be withheld as mixed. More robust
column/layout analysis would be a future feature, not a reason to guess today.

Discovery provides `known_groups` to encourage consistent names. An assigned label
must occur exactly at an identifier boundary in source text, be printable, trimmed,
and at most 200 characters. This prevents fabricated or partial identifiers but
does not decide whether host, service or business process is the right grouping
level. The model makes that choice. Review wrong groupings in the manifest before
changing the segmentation rules.

Statuses: `assigned` requires a valid target; `mixed`, `uncertain` and `unmatched`
require null target. No duplicate assignment to two outputs is permitted. Confidence
is displayed for review; there is no confidence threshold implying correctness.

## 8. Chat behavior and the reported failure

The reported message was “Abstained: the selected evidence did not support a
reliable answer,” for “has the process relay-44 started and stopped?” This message
came from a schema-valid model response with `abstain: true`; it was not a network
exception or an HTTP error. The previous code discarded the model's answer and
showed a generic abstention. An unsupported model explanation must not become a
trusted factual answer just because it is attached to an abstention.

Offline inspection of the latest saved run found a group containing explicit stop
records for two services, plus health/maintenance records, without an explicit start
record. This explains why the old instruction to abstain when evidence was missing
could suppress the entire multipart answer. It does not prove the live model's
internal reason. A useful grounded answer confirms the documented stop and states
that the supplied text does not establish the start; it does not infer “never
started,” nor conflate multiple services under one host.

The revised generic instruction answers supported parts of multipart questions,
identifies unsupported parts, distinguishes services within a group, and does not
regard start/stop at different times as inherently contradictory. It contains no
special-case process names or operation-response shortcuts.

Additional robustness changes:

- Every question re-reads only the selected process HTML and validates it.
- Chat labels parent identifiers `parent_block_id`; citations should use text `id`.
- A parent ID returned by a model can be normalized only when section, page and
  exact quote identify exactly one text line in the current request. Ambiguity fails.
- Invalid citation semantics get one bounded correction request. Invalid quotes,
  foreign IDs or pages still cannot pass. Raw failed outputs are not printed.
- Recent history is limited to four turns and at most one quarter of the budget.
  If older history is omitted, the CLI tells the user; evidence is not omitted.
- When the selected process is too large, every original unit is visited in bounded
  batches. A section can be split at unit boundaries. Every request stays within
  the configured serialized character budget and leaves room for corrections.
- Batch answers are explicitly labeled, individually cited and displayed together.
  They are not synthesized into an unsupported global conclusion. A missing fact
  in one batch does not mean it is absent from other batches. Cross-batch conflicts
  and chronology require reviewing the returned evidence; this is a deliberate
  limitation of the small implementation.
- Any invalid batch causes the entire question to abstain. API failures propagate
  without presenting a partial result as success. No failed batch is silently skipped.
- An individual huge line/question still produces an actionable budget error;
  it is never silently truncated. Use `/clear`, a smaller question, or a suitable
  higher budget. `/chunk N` remains an explicit smaller scope with separate history.

History keys are `(target, chunk)`. Different process chats, full process chat and
section chat do not share histories. Answers and history are memory-only. All
factual answers require nonempty validated citations. An image reference is never
a valid quoted text citation. Abstaining outputs are replaced with local guidance
rather than displaying unchecked assertions from the model.

## 9. HTTP and structured result handling

Only `Client` uses HTTP. `request()` enforces consent, allowed URLs, auth headers,
no redirects, ten-second connection timeout and ninety-second read timeout.
Requests has no application-level automatic retry here. Provider bodies and raw
transport exceptions are suppressed to avoid exposing secrets or source text.

`complete()` constructs system/user messages, embeds the JSON schema, requests a
result instance, sets temperature zero and output limit 2048, then parses the
Chat Completions envelope. A single enclosing JSON fence is tolerated. Arbitrary
prose around JSON, nonfinite numbers, extra fields and wrong types are rejected.
JSON/schema failure receives one correction; truncated output and transport errors
do not. Chat citation repair is a separate semantic stage, so a batch can consume
up to four HTTP completions when both repairs are needed. Classification gets at
most two completions per eligible section. Budget checks precede each request.

## 10. Output and terminal operation

| Artifact | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `source.html` | All converted evidence plus audited furniture |
| `process-NN-<slug>.html` | Only the selected target's assigned original units |
| `withheld.html` | Everything not assigned |
| `furniture.html` | Explicitly recognized non-evidence text, retained for audit |
| `index.html` | Links and classification decision table |
| `manifest.json` | Full original provenance and decisions; no credentials |
| `images/*.png` | Locally extracted image references |

Blank areas in a process HTML preserve source positioning where other evidence was
removed. Text spans/raster images are retained; fonts, vector drawings, arbitrary
rotations and complicated transforms are not guaranteed pixel-perfect.

Menu: number/name selects a group, `w` inspects withheld, `r` shows decisions, `i`
opens index, `/ls` lists files. Within a group: `/evidence`, `/images [number or ID]`,
`/html`, `/chunk N`, `/all`, `/clear`, `/back`, `/process`, `/help`, `/quit`.
`/ls` uses Python filesystem listing; it does not execute untrusted shell strings.

Ghostty/Kitty use Kitty graphics; iTerm2/WezTerm use OSC 1337. Redirected stdout and
unsupported terminals receive paths instead. Multiplexers may block protocols;
use a directly supported terminal or a deliberately configured override. Missing
images produce local errors. Citation-associated images display after answers
without sending pixels to the API. A successful escape write is not proof of
visual display; verify on the actual user's terminal.

## 11. Troubleshooting decision table

| Symptom | Check and recovery |
| --- | --- |
| Model abstains | `/evidence`: is the requested fact present as text in this selected group? Try a specific supported part. Check screenshots via `/images`. Review grouping and missing PDF slices. Do not remove validation to force answers. |
| Unsupported/foreign citations | Confirm line IDs and exact quotes; one repair is automatic. Repeated failures suggest model/prompt compatibility; inspect a sanitized synthetic reproduction. |
| Context budget error | `/clear`; shorten question; choose `/chunk N`; increase `--context-chars` only within actual model capacity. Check for exceptionally long individual lines. |
| HTTP 401 / 403 | Check router versus provider key and `--auth`; verify access. Never print the key. |
| HTTP 404 | Correct complete route, base prefix and exact model ID. |
| HTTP 400 | Check structured response support, model parameters and context limits. Try the explicitly supported JSON mode. |
| HTTP 429 | Stop; inspect quota/rate limits and retry later or choose available model. |
| Redirect refused | Enter the final trusted endpoint directly. |
| Network/TLS failure | Confirm endpoint, certificates, proxy and reachability; do not disable TLS checks. |
| Truncated model output | Reduce scope or choose appropriate model/output capacity; inspect 2048-token setting in `complete()`. |
| Invalid JSON/schema after repair | Provider may echo schema or add fields; use synthetic data to evaluate compatibility. |
| HTML modified/missing/foreign | Reconvert source. Do not bypass integrity checks or use another process's HTML. |
| Wrong groups | Inspect decisions and original text; adjust generic segmentation/prompt or provide explicit payload. |
| All image-only pages withheld | Expected without OCR; add a tested local OCR stage only as a separate requirement. |
| Image path but no graphic | Terminal/protocol limitation, not LLM failure; use compatible terminal or local viewer. |
| Generic local failure | Check PDF validity, output write permission, disk, dependency versions; reproduce using synthetic fixture under a debugger without real keys. |

Collect for an issue: version/commit, OS/Python, command flags without secrets,
question, selected label, safe error text, source section/page IDs, run state and
a sanitized small reproduction. Do not attach production PDFs, manifests or raw
HTTP headers by default. Ask the owner which evidence can be shared.

## 12. Tests and maintenance workflow

```sh
.venv/bin/python -B -m unittest -v test_pipeline
.venv/bin/python test_pipeline.py --make-fixture /tmp/evidence-fixture.pdf
```

An earlier Python snapshot passed **44 tests**; the final allocation edits were not retested. Tests create actual PDFs in
temporary directories and mock only HTTP. They cover
mixed pages, cross-page sections, repeated rows, source offsets, all-block
conservation, image ownership, real HTML export/read-back, modified/missing HTML,
provider errors, authentication modes, URL/query composition, malformed JSON,
schema errors, invalid IDs/status pairs, quotation validation, isolation, explicit
chunk scope, batch coverage and history bounds. Graphics tests inspect protocol
bytes, not a screenshot of terminal rendering. CLI tests prove automatic discovery
has no target-name prompt. The mock oracle is test data, not a production classifier.

For a segmentation change, first create a PDF fixture showing the failing layout;
assert every unit appears exactly once in sections/furniture and no process acquires
foreign evidence. For a chat change, inspect serialized mock request bodies and
assert no other process, image bytes or paths are sent. Include accepted and
rejected citations, not just a golden successful response. For a provider change,
mock HTTP errors/redirects and response envelopes before a live synthetic smoke test.

Run the full Python suite after shared backend edits. Record the exact test result,
not a copied historical count. A passed mock suite does not establish live model
accuracy. Live reproduction of the reported abstention requires the user's provider
and approved synthetic/sanitized data; no key was available during this handover.

## 13. Operations, upgrades, and next-intern checklist

1. Start by reading the short README and this guide, then run all tests.
2. Make one local extraction and inspect text, images, order, page numbering and
   withheld boundaries against the original PDF.
3. Use a controlled synthetic PDF with known outcomes for the first provider test.
   Record model ID, response mode and reviewed expected answers without secrets.
4. Test a compound question where one part is missing: confirm the supported part
   is answered with quotes and the missing part is not invented.
5. Test an unsupported question and foreign image/citation request: expect refusal.
6. Check terminal graphics on the deployment terminal and fallback behavior.
7. Measure extraction, export and model wait separately. Do not attribute network
   latency to Python or assume a language rewrite improves provider responses.
8. Review dependency upgrades one at a time. Keep pinned versions and regenerate
   fixtures only intentionally; extraction changes can alter unit IDs/boundaries.
9. Back up source PDFs and required run directories according to the organization's
   policy. Delete old runs explicitly when permitted; the app has no retention job.
10. Retain the previous code/environment for rollback; new code should use a new
    run. Never mix old manifests with regenerated HTML or images.

Known extension points: local OCR with provenance, more general layout segmentation,
explicit grouping-level configuration, resume/import validation, cross-batch
synthesis with conflict verification, context token counting per model, safe
structured diagnostics, measured accuracy evaluation, transactional exports, and
controlled parallel classification for fixed targets. They are future work, not
features to imply already exist. Keep the current minimal design until a measured
problem warrants additional infrastructure.

"""Interactive terminal frontend. Run `python cli.py --help` for local-only options."""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import base64
from datetime import datetime
from getpass import getpass
import os
import re
from pathlib import Path
import shlex
import subprocess
import tempfile
import sys
import time

import pymupdf

from rich.console import Console
from rich.progress import Progress, SpinnerColumn, TextColumn, BarColumn, MofNCompleteColumn, TimeElapsedColumn
from rich.table import Table

from pipeline import (Chat, Client, PipelineError, classify, export, extract, image_for_process,
                      load_targets, selected_sections, validate_pdf, discovered_targets)

CONSOLE = Console(markup=False, highlight=False)


def safe(text):
    """Prevent source evidence or model text from injecting terminal control sequences."""
    return ''.join(c for c in str(text) if c in '\n\t' or (ord(c) >= 32 and ord(c) != 127))


def say(text):
    """Print untrusted text literally, including square brackets."""
    CONSOLE.print(safe(text))


def prompt(label, default=''):
    """Read a visible value with an optional default; Ctrl-C cancels safely."""
    result = input(f'{label}' + (f' [{default}]' if default else '') + ': ').strip()
    return result or default


def pdf_path(value: str) -> Path:
    """Resolve typed, quoted or shell-escaped drag-and-drop paths without a shell."""
    value = value.strip()
    if not value:
        raise PipelineError('Enter a PDF path.')
    # Try literal paths first so real quotes/backslashes in filenames are preserved.
    literal = Path(value).expanduser()
    if literal.is_file():
        return validate_pdf(literal)
    if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in ('"', "'"):
        unquoted = Path(value[1:-1]).expanduser()
        if unquoted.is_file():
            return validate_pdf(unquoted)
    if os.name != 'nt':
        try:
            parts = shlex.split(value)
        except ValueError:
            raise PipelineError('The path has unmatched quotes. Drag the PDF again or type its full path.') from None
        if len(parts) == 1:
            return validate_pdf(Path(parts[0]))
    raise PipelineError('Choose one existing PDF file. Drag it into the terminal or type its path.')


def select_pdf(value=None):
    """Validate a command-line path or let an interactive user correct their input."""
    if value is not None:
        return pdf_path(value)
    while True:
        try:
            return pdf_path(prompt('Drag and drop a PDF here, or type its path'))
        except PipelineError as error:
            say(f'Error: {error}')


def image_protocol():
    """Select a native graphics protocol, with an override for unrecognized terminals."""
    if not sys.stdout.isatty():
        return None
    override = os.environ.get('EVIDENCE_IMAGE_PROTOCOL', 'auto').lower()
    if override in ('kitty', 'iterm2'):
        return override
    if override == 'none' or os.environ.get('TMUX') or os.environ.get('STY'):
        return None
    terminal = os.environ.get('TERM', '').lower()
    program = os.environ.get('TERM_PROGRAM', '').lower()
    if ('kitty' in terminal or 'ghostty' in terminal or program in ('kitty', 'ghostty') or
            os.environ.get('KITTY_WINDOW_ID')):
        return 'kitty'
    if program in ('iterm.app', 'wezterm') or os.environ.get('WEZTERM_PANE'):
        return 'iterm2'
    return None


def supports_images():
    """Report whether the current output supports a detected native image protocol."""
    return image_protocol() is not None


def preview_pdf(source: Path):
    """Show local PDF details and a first-page preview before collecting credentials."""
    try:
        with pymupdf.open(source) as document:
            if document.needs_pass:
                raise PipelineError('Encrypted PDF: provide an unlocked local copy.')
            if not document.is_pdf or not len(document):
                raise PipelineError('Choose a PDF containing at least one page.')
            page = document[0]
            say(f'\nPDF preview: {source.name}\nLocation: {source.resolve()}\n'
                f'Pages: {len(document)} | Size: {source.stat().st_size / 1024:.1f} KB')
            text = page.get_text(sort=True).strip()
            say('First-page text excerpt (local extraction, up to 800 characters):')
            say(text[:800] + ('\n[Preview shortened]' if len(text) > 800 else '')
                if text else 'No extractable text on the first page.')
            if supports_images():
                # Preview pixels remain local and temporary, never part of LLM context.
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='pdf-preview-') as folder:
                    image = Path(folder) / 'first-page.png'
                    scale = min(1.0, 900 / max(page.rect.width, page.rect.height))
                    page.get_pixmap(matrix=pymupdf.Matrix(scale, scale), alpha=False).save(image)
                    show_image(image)
            else:
                say('This terminal cannot show a visual PDF preview inline.')
                if sys.stdin.isatty() and prompt('Open the PDF in the system viewer for a visual preview? [y/N]', 'n').lower() == 'y':
                    open_local(source)
    except (OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError):
        raise PipelineError('Cannot preview this PDF. Check that it is readable and not damaged.') from None


def configure(auth='bearer', mode='json_object', endpoint_kind='auto'):
    """Collect an in-memory key, exact model ID and explicit disclosure consent."""
    endpoint = prompt('API base URL or full chat-completion URL (HTTPS)')
    if not sys.stdin.isatty():
        raise PipelineError('Use an interactive terminal for hidden API-key entry, or run --extract-only.')
    key = getpass('API key or token (hidden; never saved): ')
    model = prompt('Model ID, or Enter for router auto-selection (/models lists models)', 'auto')
    say('Classification and chat send evidence to the configured API, which may forward to upstream providers: '
        'extracted document text and selected-group conversation text. Images stay local.')
    if prompt('Approve this disclosure to the configured service? Type YES') != 'YES':
        raise PipelineError('Disclosure not approved. Use --extract-only to inspect local conversion.')
    client = Client(endpoint, key, model, mode=mode, consent=True, endpoint_kind=endpoint_kind, auth=auth)
    if model == '/models':
        with CONSOLE.status('Loading model catalog…'):
            catalog = client.models()
        if not catalog:
            raise PipelineError('No callable models listed. Check provider access/quota or enter an exact model ID.')
        for number, name in enumerate(catalog, 1):
            say(f'{number}. {name}')
        selection = prompt('Exact model ID from this catalog')
        if selection not in catalog:
            raise PipelineError('Select an exact listed model ID.')
        client.model = selection
    elif model in ('auto', '/auto'):
        client.model = 'auto'
        say('Service auto routing selected explicitly; this requires support from the configured API.')
    elif not model:
        raise PipelineError('Enter an exact model ID or explicitly choose /auto.')
    say(f'Configured model route: {client.model}')
    return client


def stage(label, function):
    """Show real completed-unit counts and elapsed time, plus an indeterminate spinner."""
    with Progress(SpinnerColumn(), TextColumn('{task.description}'), BarColumn(),
                  MofNCompleteColumn(), TimeElapsedColumn(), console=CONSOLE) as progress:
        task = progress.add_task(label, total=None)

        def update(done, total):
            """Advance only after the backend completes a page or section."""
            progress.update(task, completed=done, total=total)

        return function(update)


def results(data, decisions):
    """Show every decision with original pages and an uncalibrated confidence estimate."""
    table = Table(title='Routing results — confidence is a model estimate')
    for heading in ('Section', 'Pages', 'Process', 'Status', 'Confidence', 'Reason'):
        table.add_column(heading, overflow='fold')
    sections = {s['id']: s for s in data['sections']}
    for d in decisions:
        pages = sorted({b['page'] for b in sections[d['section_id']]['blocks']})
        table.add_row(d['section_id'], ','.join(map(str, pages)), safe(d['target'] or '—'),
                      d['status'], f'{d["confidence"]:.2f}', safe(d['reason']))
    CONSOLE.print(table)


def evidence(sections):
    """Print complete source text and image references with section/block/page citations."""
    for number, section in enumerate(sections, 1):
        say(f'\nChunk {number}: {section["id"]}')
        for b in section['blocks']:
            say(f'  [{b["id"]}, source page {b["page"]}] {b["text"] if b["kind"] == "text" else "LOCAL IMAGE"}')


def show_image(path: Path, offer_viewer=True):
    """Display locally through Kitty/iTerm2; otherwise offer a default-viewer fallback."""
    if not path.is_file():
        raise PipelineError('Image file is missing. Reconvert the source PDF.')
    protocol = image_protocol()
    if protocol == 'kitty':
        encoded = base64.b64encode(path.read_bytes()).decode()
        for start in range(0, len(encoded), 4096):
            chunk = encoded[start:start + 4096]
            more = int(start + 4096 < len(encoded))
            control = f'a=T,f=100,q=2,m={more}' if start == 0 else f'm={more}'
            sys.stdout.write(f'\033_G{control};{chunk}\033\\')
        sys.stdout.flush()
    elif protocol == 'iterm2':
        encoded = base64.b64encode(path.read_bytes()).decode()
        sys.stdout.write(f'\033]1337;File=inline=1;preserveAspectRatio=1:{encoded}\a\n')
        sys.stdout.flush()
    else:
        say(f'Inline image display is unavailable in this terminal. Local image: {path}')
        say('Use Ghostty, Kitty, iTerm2 or WezTerm for native inline images.')
        if offer_viewer and sys.stdin.isatty() and prompt('Open in the system image viewer? [y/N]', 'n').lower() == 'y':
            open_local(path)


def open_local(path):
    """Open one existing local file without a shell or any LLM call."""
    if not path.is_file():
        raise PipelineError('The selected local file is missing.')
    if sys.platform == 'darwin':
        command = ['open', str(path.resolve())]
    elif sys.platform == 'win32':
        os.startfile(path.resolve())
        return
    else:
        command = ['xdg-open', str(path.resolve())]
    try:
        subprocess.run(command, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    except (OSError, subprocess.CalledProcessError):
        raise PipelineError('Cannot launch the default viewer. Open the displayed local path manually.') from None


def images(data, decisions, target, run, selection=None):
    """Choose a reference by number or ID and display it without calling the LLM."""
    references = []
    for section in selected_sections(data, decisions, target):
        for index, block in enumerate(section['blocks']):
            if block['kind'] == 'image':
                caption = next((b['text'] for b in section['blocks'][index + 1:]
                                if b['kind'] == 'text'), '(no following caption)')
                references.append(block)
                say(f'{len(references)}. {block["id"]} | page {block["page"]} | {caption}')
    if not references:
        say('No image references in this selection.')
        return
    selection = selection or prompt('Image number or ID (blank to return)')
    if selection:
        if selection.isdigit():
            number = int(selection)
            if not 1 <= number <= len(references):
                raise PipelineError('Choose a listed image number.')
            selection = references[number - 1]['id']
        show_image(image_for_process(data, decisions, target, selection, run))


def answer_images(data, decisions, target, run, answer):
    """Display source images attached to cited sections, scoped to the current group."""
    cited = {c['section_id'] for c in answer['citations']}
    for section in selected_sections(data, decisions, target):
        if section['id'] not in cited:
            continue
        for block in section['blocks']:
            if block['kind'] == 'image':
                say(f'Reference image: source page {block["page"]} | {block["id"]}')
                show_image(image_for_process(data, decisions, target, block['id'], run), offer_viewer=False)


def list_process_html(manifest, run):
    """Provide a portable /ls view of every target HTML and its routing status."""
    say(f'Process HTML files — run status: {manifest["state"]}')
    if not manifest['documents']:
        say('No groups assigned yet. Inspect withheld.html for unclassified evidence.')
    for target, name in manifest['documents'].items():
        path = run / name
        count = sum(d['status'] == 'assigned' and d['target'] == target for d in manifest['decisions'])
        detail = f'{path.stat().st_size:,} bytes' if path.is_file() else 'MISSING'
        say(f'{name} | {target} | {count} assigned sections | {detail} | {path.resolve()}')


def browse(data, decisions, targets, manifest, run, client):
    """Expose every process and withheld document, plus isolated process chat commands."""
    chat = Chat(data, decisions, client, run, manifest['documents']) if client else None
    while True:
        say('\nDocuments:')
        for number, target in enumerate(targets, 1):
            count = len(selected_sections(data, decisions, target))
            say(f'{number}. {target} ({count} sections) — {run / manifest["documents"][target]}')
        say('w. Withheld evidence   r. Results   /ls. List HTML files   i. Open index   q. Quit')
        choice = prompt('Select document')
        if choice in ('q', '/quit'):
            return
        if choice == '/ls':
            list_process_html(manifest, run)
            continue
        if choice == 'r':
            results(data, decisions)
            continue
        if choice == 'i':
            open_local(run / 'index.html')
            continue
        if choice == 'w':
            target = None
        elif choice in targets:
            target = choice
        elif choice.isdigit() and 1 <= int(choice) <= len(targets):
            target = targets[int(choice) - 1]
        else:
            say('Choose a listed document number or command.')
            continue
        chunk = None
        say(f'\nSelected: {target or "Withheld evidence"}. /help lists commands.')
        while True:
            command = prompt('Question or command')
            try:
                if command in ('/back', '/process'):
                    break
                if command == '/quit':
                    return
                if command == '/help':
                    say('/ls: list process HTML files | /evidence: source text and chunk IDs | /images [number]: local images | /html: open document\n'
                        '/chunk N: explicitly limit chat to one section | /all: full process | /clear: reset this chat history\n'
                        '/process or /back: switch documents | /quit: exit')
                elif command == '/ls':
                    list_process_html(manifest, run)
                elif command == '/evidence':
                    evidence(selected_sections(data, decisions, target))
                elif command == '/images' or command.startswith('/images ') or re.fullmatch(
                        r'(?:show|display)(?: me)?(?: the)?(?: reference| evidence)? (?:images?|screenshots?)(?: please)?', command, re.I):
                    selection = command.split(maxsplit=1)[1] if command.startswith('/images ') else None
                    images(data, decisions, target, run, selection)
                elif command == '/html':
                    open_local(run / (manifest['documents'][target] if target else 'withheld.html'))
                elif command.startswith('/chunk '):
                    number = command.split(maxsplit=1)[1]
                    if not number.isdigit() or not 1 <= int(number) <= len(selected_sections(data, decisions, target)):
                        raise PipelineError('Use /evidence to see valid chunk numbers, then /chunk N.')
                    chunk = int(number)
                    say(f'Chat now covers ONLY section chunk {chunk}; other sections are excluded explicitly.')
                elif command == '/all':
                    chunk = None
                    say('Chat now covers the full selected process.')
                elif command == '/clear':
                    if chat:
                        chat.histories.pop((target, chunk), None)
                    say('Selected chat history cleared.')
                elif command.startswith('/'):
                    say('Unknown command. Use /help.')
                elif command:
                    if target is None or chat is None:
                        say('Chat requires a classified process and an approved model connection.')
                        continue
                    with CONSOLE.status('Waiting for a cited text answer…'):
                        answer = chat.ask(target, command, chunk)
                    if chat.last_notice:
                        say(chat.last_notice.strip())
                    say(answer['answer'])
                    for citation in answer['citations']:
                        say(f'{citation["section_id"]} / {citation["block_id"]}, source page {citation["page"]}: "{citation["quote"]}"')
                    answer_images(data, decisions, target, run, answer)
            except PipelineError as error:
                say(f'Error: {error}')


def main():
    """Run the workflow; preserve a local review export before any network classification."""
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--pdf', help='Path to one PDF file (or enter/drag it at the prompt)')
    parser.add_argument('--payload', type=Path, help='Optional fixed target labels; otherwise the LLM discovers groups')
    parser.add_argument('--auth', choices=['bearer', 'api-key'], default='bearer', help='Advanced: authentication header')
    parser.add_argument('--response-format', choices=['json_object', 'json_schema'], default='json_object', help='Advanced: provider JSON mode')
    parser.add_argument('--url-type', choices=['auto', 'base', 'chat'], default='auto', help='Advanced: API URL interpretation')
    parser.add_argument('--context-chars', type=int, default=24000, help='Serialized request character budget (not tokens)')
    parser.add_argument('--extract-only', action='store_true', help='Convert and export locally; never configure or call a model')
    parser.add_argument('--no-browser', action='store_true', help='Exit after writing output files')
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.context_chars < 4000:
        raise PipelineError('--context-chars must be at least 4000.')
    say('Evidence Splitter\nChoose PDF → preview → configure text model → convert → discover groups → inspect → chat/view images')
    start = time.monotonic()
    source = select_pdf(args.pdf)
    preview_pdf(source)
    targets = load_targets(args.payload.expanduser()) if args.payload else None
    client = None if args.extract_only else configure(args.auth, args.response_format, args.url_type)
    if client:
        client.budget = args.context_chars
    say('The LLM will discover process groups from the PDF text.' if targets is None
        else 'Routing to the labels in the supplied payload.')
    run = Path('runs') / datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d-%H%M%S-%f')
    say(f'Output folder: {run.resolve()}')
    data = stage('Converting pages', lambda progress: extract(source, run, progress))
    say(f'Extracted {len(data["pages"])} pages and {len(data["sections"])} sections.')
    decisions = [dict(section_id=s['id'], status='uncertain', target=None, confidence=0,
                      reason=s['boundary'] or 'Not classified; local extraction only.') for s in data['sections']]
    manifest = export(data, targets or [], decisions, run, state='not classified')
    if client:
        try:
            decisions = stage('Discovering groups' if targets is None else 'Classifying sections', lambda progress: classify(data, targets, client, run, progress))
        except (PipelineError, KeyboardInterrupt):
            say(f'Classification stopped. Local evidence remains available at {run.resolve() / "index.html"}. No partial routing was published.')
            raise
        targets = discovered_targets(decisions) if targets is None else targets
        manifest = export(data, targets, decisions, run)
    results(data, decisions)
    list_process_html(manifest, run)
    say(f'Finished in {time.monotonic() - start:.1f}s. Review: {run.resolve() / "index.html"}')
    if not args.no_browser:
        browse(data, decisions, list(manifest['documents']), manifest, run, client)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    try:
        main()
    except (KeyboardInterrupt, EOFError):
        say('\nCancelled. Any completed local exports remain in runs/.')
    except PipelineError as error:
        say(f'Error: {error}')
        sys.exit(1)
    except Exception:
        # Never emit a traceback that might include provider credentials or content.
        say('Local operation failed. Check the input PDF, output permissions and installed requirements. No provider details were logged.')
        sys.exit(1)
